\displaystyle|h|_{(X^{k_{0}+1+\sigma})^{3}}\leq\frac{c}{s}\left((1+\left|R\right|_{(X^{k_{0}+4+\sigma})^{3}})\left|z\right|_{Y^{k_{0}+1}\times(Y^{k_{0}})^{2}}+\left|z\right|_{Y^{k_{0}+1+\sigma}\times(Y^{k_{0}+\sigma})^{2}}\right).

Combining with (
4.55
), we obtain (
4.29
).

Proof of (C).
Finally, if
R=0
, then we have
|\partial_{\Theta}G|_{Y^{k_{0}+1}\times(Y^{k_{0}})^{2}}=|\partial_{\Theta}G(\Theta_{3},sv)|_{{Y^{k_{0}+1}\times(Y^{k_{0}})^{2}}}\leq cs
. Therefore we can improve (
4.54
) and obtain

\displaystyle|h|_{(X^{k_{0}+1})^{3}}\leq c|z|_{Y^{k_{0}+1}\times(Y^{k_{0}})^{2}},

which implies (
4.31
). (
4.27
) follows trivially from (
4.30
). This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 4.10

Let
\tilde{G}_{s}
be as in (
4.19
). Then there exists an open set
V^{3}
near
0\in(X^{3})^{3}
such that for all
R,\tilde{R}\in V^{3}
and
k\geq 2
,

(a’)

(Initial value)
|\tilde{G}_{s}(0)|_{(X^{k})^{3}}\lesssim_{k}s^{2}.

(b’)

(Taylor estimate) If
\rVert R\rVert_{(X^{k+3})^{3}},\rVert\tilde{R}\rVert_{(X^{k+3})^{3}}\leq\eta
for some
0<\eta=\eta(k)<1
, then

\displaystyle\begin{cases}|D\tilde{G}_{s}(R)[h]|_{(Y^{k})^{3}}\lesssim_{k}|h|_{(X^{k+1})^{3}}\\
|\tilde{G}_{s}(\tilde{R})-\tilde{G}_{s}(R)-D\tilde{G}_{s}(R)[\tilde{R}-R]|_{(Y^{k})^{3}}\lesssim_{k}|\tilde{R}-R|_{(X^{k+1})^{3}}^{2}\\
|\tilde{G}_{s}(\tilde{R})-\tilde{G}_{s}(R)-D\tilde{G}_{s}(R)[\tilde{R}-R]-\frac{1}{2}D^{2}\tilde{G}_{s}[\tilde{R}-R,\tilde{R}-R]|_{(Y^{k})^{3}}\lesssim_{k}|\tilde{R}-R|_{(X^{k+1})^{3}}^{3}.\end{cases}

Proof.

(a’) follows from the fact that
v\in\text{Ker}(DG(\Theta_{3}^{*},0))
and
G(\Theta_{3}^{*},0)=0
. (b’) is due to (b) in Theorem
4.7
.
∎

4.3.2
Nash-Moser iteration

For
\beta,N>0
and
2\leq k\in\mathbb{N}
, we consider a regularizing operator
S(N):(X^{k})^{3}\mapsto(C^{\infty})^{3}
such that

\displaystyle\begin{cases}|S(N)R|_{(X^{k+\beta})^{3}}\lesssim_{k,\beta}N^{\beta}|R|_{(X^{k})^{3}}&\forall\ R\in(H^{k})^{3}\\
|(I-S(N))R|_{(H^{k})^{3}}\lesssim_{k,\beta}N^{-\beta}|R|_{(X^{k+\beta})^{3}}&\forall\ R\in(H^{k+\beta})^{3}.\end{cases}

(4.57)

Note that we can choose
S
so that
PS(N)=S(N)P
, since
v
is supported on the
m
-th Fourier mode (see (d) in Theorem
4.7
).

For
N_{n}>1
and even integer
\beta>0
, which will be chosen later, we set

\displaystyle\begin{cases}R_{n+1}=R_{n}-S(N_{n})T_{s}(R_{n})^{-1}[\tilde{G}_{s}(R_{n})]&\text{ for }n\geq 0\\
R_{0}=0,\end{cases}

(4.58)

and

\displaystyle\begin{cases}a_{n}:=|R_{n+1}-R_{n}|_{(X^{k_{0}+1})^{3}}\\
a^{\prime}_{n}:=|R_{n+1}-R_{n}|_{(X^{k_{0}+2})^{3}}\\
a^{\prime\prime}_{n}=|R_{n+1}-R_{n}|_{(X^{k_{0}+4})^{3}}\\
b_{n}:=|\tilde{G}_{s}(R_{n})|_{(Y^{k_{0}})^{3}}\\
C_{n}:=|T_{s}(R_{n})^{-1}[\tilde{G}_{s}(R_{n})]|_{(X^{k_{0}+1+\beta})^{3}}.\end{cases}

(4.59)

Note that our goal is to show that
\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}a_{n}<\infty
, which implies that
R_{n}
converges in
(X^{k_{0}+1})^{3}
. In order to prove the assumptions in (
4.22
)-(
4.24
), we will need the boundedness of
a_{n}^{\prime}
and
a_{n}^{\prime\prime}
as well.

Lemma 4.11

Let
\epsilon>0
and
k_{0}\geq 2
fixed and let
s_{0}
and
\delta
be defined as in Lemma
4.9
. We also assume that
\delta
is even smaller if necessary, so that
\delta<\eta(k_{0})
where
\eta(k_{0})
is as in (b’) in Lemma
4.10
. For any
n\geq 0
such that
R_{n}
satisfies (
4.22
), (
4.23
) and (
4.24
) for some
s\in(0,s_{0})
, then
we have

\displaystyle a_{0}\lesssim_{\epsilon,k_{0}}s,

(4.60)

\displaystyle a_{n}\lesssim_{\epsilon,k_{0}}\frac{1}{s}N_{n}b_{n}

\displaystyle\text{ for }n\geq 1,

(4.61)

\displaystyle b_{0}\lesssim_{\epsilon,k_{0},\beta}s^{2}

(4.62)

\displaystyle b_{1}\lesssim_{\epsilon,k_{0},\beta}(N_{0}^{3}+1)s^{3}+N_{0}^{-\beta}C_{0}.

(4.63)

\displaystyle b_{n+1}\lesssim_{\epsilon,k_{0},\beta}a_{n}^{2}+\frac{1}{s}N_{n}b_{n}^{2}+N_{n}^{-\beta}(1+b_{n})C_{n}

\displaystyle\text{ for }n\geq 1,

(4.64)

\displaystyle a^{\prime}_{n}\lesssim_{\epsilon,k_{0}}\frac{1}{s}N_{n}^{2}b_{n}

\displaystyle\text{ for }n\geq 0,

(4.65)

\displaystyle a^{\prime\prime}_{n}\lesssim_{\epsilon,k_{0}}\frac{1}{s}N_{n}^{4}b_{n}

\displaystyle\text{ for }n\geq 0.

(4.66)

Furthermore, we have

\displaystyle|PR_{1}|_{(X^{k_{0}+2})^{3}}\lesssim_{\epsilon,k_{0}}N_{0}s

(4.67)

\displaystyle|(I-P)R_{1}|_{(X^{k_{0}+2})^{3}}\lesssim_{\epsilon,k_{0}}N_{0}s^{2}.

(4.68)

Proof.

Proof of (
4.60
), (
4.61
), (
4.67
) and (
4.68
).
We first claim that

\displaystyle|PR_{1}|_{(X^{k_{0}+1})^{3}}\lesssim s

(4.69)

\displaystyle|(I-P)R_{1}|_{(X^{k_{0}+1})^{3}}\lesssim s^{2},

(4.70)

Let us prove (
4.69
) first. Thanks to (a’) in Lemma
4.10
and (
4.30
), we have

\displaystyle|PR_{1}|_{(X^{k_{0}+1})^{3}}=|S(N_{0})PT_{s}(0)^{-1}[\tilde{G}_{s}(0)]|_{(X^{k_{0}+1})^{3}}\lesssim|PT_{s}(0)^{-1}[\tilde{G}_{s}(0)]|_{(X^{k_{0}+1})^{3}}\lesssim\frac{1}{s}|\tilde{G}_{s}(0)|_{(Y^{k_{0}}+1)^{3}}\lesssim s,

where we used that
S(N_{0})
and
P
commute to obtain the first equality. To prove (
4.70
), we use (
4.31
) and compute

\displaystyle|(I-P)R_{1}|_{(X^{k_{0}+1})^{3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim|S(N_{0})(I-P)T_{s}(0)^{-1}[\tilde{G}_{s}(0)]|_{(X^{k_{0}+1})^{3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim|(I-P)T_{s}(0)^{-1}[\tilde{G}_{s}(0)]|_{(X^{k_{0}+1})^{3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim|\tilde{G}_{s}(0)|_{(Y^{k_{0}+1})^{3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim s^{2},

which proves (
4.70
). With the claim, (
4.60
) follows immediately. (
4.61
) (
4.67
) and (
4.68
) can be proved in exactly the same way as above using (
4.57
).

Proof of (
4.62
), (
4.63
) and (
4.64
).

Note that Lemma
4.10
immediately implies (
4.62
). Now, let us prove (
4.63
) and (
4.64
). We have

\displaystyle b_{n+1}:=|\tilde{G}_{s}(R_{n+1})|_{(Y^{k_{0}})^{3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{|\tilde{G}_{s}(R_{n+1})-\tilde{G}_{s}(R_{n})-D\tilde{G}_{s}(R_{n})[R_{n+1}-R_{n}]|_{(Y^{k_{0}})^{3}}}

\displaystyle\ +{|\tilde{G}_{s}(R_{n})+D\tilde{G}_{s}(R_{n})[R_{n+1}-R_{n}]|_{(Y^{k_{0}})^{3}}}

\displaystyle=:J_{1}+J_{2},

(4.71)

We claim that

\displaystyle\begin{cases}J_{1}\lesssim s^{3}&\text{ if }n=0\\
J_{1}\lesssim a_{n}^{2}=|R_{n+1}-R_{n}|_{(X^{k_{0}+1})^{3}}^{2}&\text{ if }n\geq 1.\end{cases}

(4.72)

If
n=0
, we have

\displaystyle J_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg|\tilde{G}_{s}({R_{1}})-\tilde{G}_{s}(0)-D\tilde{G}_{s}(0)[R_{1}]-\frac{1}{2}D^{2}\tilde{G}_{s}(0)\left[R_{1},R_{1}\right]\bigg|_{(Y^{k_{0}})^{3}}+\frac{1}{2}|D^{2}\tilde{G}_{s}(0)[R_{1},R_{1}]|_{(Y^{k_{0}})^{3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim|R_{1}|^{3}_{(X^{k_{0}+1})^{3}}+\frac{1}{2}|D^{2}\tilde{G}_{s}(0)[R_{1},R_{1}]|_{(Y^{k_{0}})^{3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim s^{3}+\frac{1}{2}|D^{2}\tilde{G}_{s}(0)[R_{1},R_{1}]|_{(Y^{k_{0}})^{3}},

where we used (b’) in Lemma
4.10
in the second inequality and (
4.69
) and (
4.70
) in the last inequality.
To estimate
D^{2}\tilde{G}
, we recall the definition of
\tilde{G}_{s}
and compute

\displaystyle D^{2}\tilde{G}_{s}(0)[R_{1},R_{1}]

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{d}{dt}\right)^{2}G(\Theta_{3}^{*}+tv\cdot R_{1},sv+(I-P)tR_{1})\bigg|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=\partial_{\Theta\Theta}G(\Theta_{3}^{*},sv)(v\cdot R_{1})^{2}+2(v\cdot R_{1})\partial_{\Theta}DG(\Theta_{3}^{*},sv)[(I-P)[R_{1}]]

\displaystyle\ +D^{2}G(\Theta_{3}^{*},sv)\left[(I-P)R_{1},(I-P)R_{1}\right].

Since
\partial_{\Theta\Theta}G(\Theta_{3}^{*},0)=0
, therefore

\left|\partial_{\Theta\Theta}G(\Theta^{*}_{3},sv)\right|_{(Y^{k_{0}})^{3}}=\left|\int_{0}^{s}\frac{d}{dt}\left(\partial_{\Theta\Theta}G(\Theta^{*}_{3},tv)\right)dt\right|_{(Y^{k_{0}})^{3}}\lesssim s,

which implies
|\partial_{\Theta\Theta}G(\Theta_{3}^{*},sv)(v\cdot R_{1})^{2}|_{(Y^{k_{0}})^{3}}\lesssim s^{3}
, since
|v\cdot R_{1}|\lesssim|PR_{1}|_{(X^{k_{0}+1})^{3}}\lesssim s
, which follows from (
4.69
). Also it follows from (b), (
4.69
) and (
4.70
) that

\displaystyle|2(v\cdot R_{1})\partial_{\Theta}DG(\Theta_{3}^{*},sv)[(I-P)[R_{1}]]|_{(Y^{k_{0}})^{3}}\lesssim s^{3},

\displaystyle|D^{2}G(\Theta_{3}^{*},sv)[(I-P)R_{1},(I-P)R_{1}]|_{(Y^{k_{0}})^{3}}\lesssim s^{4},

therefore,
|D^{2}\tilde{G}_{s}(0)[R_{1},R_{1}]|_{(Y^{k_{0}})^{3}}\lesssim s^{3}
.
Thus, we have
J_{1}\lesssim s^{3}
, which proves (
4.72
) for
n=0
. If
n\geq 1
, the claim in (
4.72
) follows immediately from (b’) in Lemma
4.10
.

In order to estimate
J_{2}
in (
4.3.2
), we have that for
n\geq 0
,

\displaystyle J_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim{|(I-D\tilde{G}_{s}(R_{n})\circ T_{s}(R_{n})^{-1})[\tilde{G}_{s}(R_{n})]|_{(Y^{k_{0}})^{3}}}

\displaystyle\ +{|D\tilde{G}_{s}(R_{n})\left(I-S(N_{n})\right)T_{s}(R_{n})^{-1}[\tilde{G}_{s}(R_{n})]|_{(Y^{k_{0}})^{3}}}

\displaystyle=:J_{21}+J_{22}.

Then it follows from (
4.57
) and (
4.28
) that

\displaystyle J_{21}

\displaystyle\lesssim|\tilde{G}_{s}(R_{n})|_{(Y^{k_{0}})^{3}}|T_{s}(R_{n})^{-1}[\tilde{G}_{s}(R_{n})]|_{(X^{k_{0}+1})^{3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim|\tilde{G}_{s}(R_{n})|_{(Y^{k_{0}})^{3}}|S(N_{n})T_{s}(R_{n})^{-1}[\tilde{G}_{s}(R_{n})]|_{(X^{k_{0}+1})^{3}}

\displaystyle\ +|\tilde{G}_{s}(R_{n})|_{(Y^{k_{0}})^{3}}|\left(I-S(N_{n})\right)T_{s}(R_{n})^{-1}[\tilde{G}_{s}(R_{n})]|_{(X^{k_{0}+1})^{3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{n}|\tilde{G}_{s}(R_{n})|_{(Y^{k_{0}})^{3}}|T_{s}(R_{n})^{-1}[\tilde{G}_{s}(R_{n})]|_{(X^{k_{0}})^{3}}

\displaystyle\ +N_{n}^{-\beta}|\tilde{G}_{s}(R_{n})|_{(Y^{k_{0}})^{3}}|T_{s}(R_{n})^{-1}[\tilde{G}_{s}(R_{n})]|_{(X^{k_{0}+1+\beta})^{3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{s}N_{n}|\tilde{G}_{s}(R_{n})|_{(Y^{k_{0}})^{3}}^{2}+N_{n}^{-\beta}|\tilde{G}_{s}(R_{n})|_{(Y^{k_{0}})^{3}}|T_{s}(R_{n})^{-1}[\tilde{G}_{s}(R_{n})]|_{(X^{k_{0}+1+\beta})^{3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{s}N_{n}b_{n}^{2}+N_{n}^{-\beta}b_{n}C_{n},

where the fourth inequality follows from (
4.32
).
Also we have

\displaystyle J_{22}\lesssim|\left(I-S(N_{n})\right)T_{s}(R_{n})^{-1}[\tilde{G}_{s}(R_{n})]|_{(X^{k_{0}+1})^{3}}\lesssim N_{n}^{-\beta}C_{n}.

Hence we have for
n\geq 0
that

\displaystyle J_{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{s}N_{n}b_{n}^{2}+N_{n}^{-\beta}(1+b_{n})C_{n}.

(4.73)

Thus with (
4.3.2
), (
4.72
), (
4.73
) and (a’) in lemma
4.10
, we have that

\displaystyle\begin{cases}b_{1}\lesssim(1+N_{0})s^{3}+N_{0}^{-\beta}C_{0}\\
b_{n+1}\lesssim a_{n}^{2}+\frac{1}{s}N_{n}b_{n}^{2}+N_{n}^{-\beta}(1+b_{n})C_{n}&\text{ for }n\geq 1.\end{cases}

(4.74)

Proof of (
4.65
) and (
4.66
).

Finally, for (
4.65
) and (
4.66
), we use (
4.32
) to compute

\displaystyle|R_{n+1}-R_{n}|_{(X^{k_{0}+2})^{3}}=|S(N_{n})T_{s}(R_{n})^{-1}[\tilde{G}_{s}(R_{n})]|_{\left(X^{k_{0}+2}\right)^{3}}\lesssim N_{n}^{2}\frac{1}{s}b_{n},

(4.75)

\displaystyle|R_{n+1}-R_{n}|_{(X^{k_{0}+4})^{3}}=|S(N_{n})T_{s}(R_{n})^{-1}[\tilde{G}_{s}(R_{n})]|_{\left(X^{k_{0}+4}\right)^{3}}\lesssim N_{n}^{4}\frac{1}{s}b_{n}.

(4.76)

This finishes the proof.
∎

Let us now derive a recursive formula for
C_{n}
.

Lemma 4.12

For
j=0,\ldots,n-1
assume that
R_{j}
satisfies the same assumptions as in Lemma
4.11
, that is, given
\epsilon>0
and
k_{0}\geq 2
,
R_{j}
satisfies (
4.22
), (
4.23
) and (
4.24
) and the assumptions of Lemma
4.10
for
s\in(0,s_{0}(\epsilon,k_{0}))
and
\delta=\delta(\epsilon,k_{0})>0
. Then,

\displaystyle C_{n}\lesssim_{\epsilon,k_{0},\beta}\frac{1}{s}\left(1+n\sup_{j=0,\ldots n-1}N_{j}^{4}C_{j}\right),\quad\text{ for $n\geq 1$ and }\quad C_{0}\lesssim_{\epsilon,k_{0},\beta}s.

Proof.

It follows from (a’) in Lemma
4.10
and (
4.29
) in Lemma
4.9
that

\displaystyle C_{0}\lesssim\frac{1}{s}|\tilde{G}_{s}(0)|_{Y^{k_{0}+1+\beta}\times(Y^{k_{0}+\beta})^{2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{s}|\tilde{G}_{s}(0)|_{(Y^{k_{0}+1+\beta})^{3}}\lesssim s.

Now let us assume that
n\geq 1
. It follows from the definition of
C_{n}
in (
4.59
) and (
4.29
) that (recall that
\beta
is even)

\displaystyle C_{n}\lesssim\frac{1}{s}\left((1+|R_{n}|_{(X^{k_{0}+4+\beta})^{3}})|\tilde{G}_{s}(R_{n})|_{Y^{k_{0}+1}\times(Y^{k_{0}})^{2}}+|\tilde{G}_{s}(R_{n})|_{Y^{k_{0}+1+\beta}\times(Y^{k_{0}+\beta})^{2}}\right).

Using (
4.8
), we have

\displaystyle|\tilde{G}_{s}(R_{n})|_{Y^{k_{0}+1}\times(Y^{k_{0}})^{2}}\lesssim|\tilde{G}_{s}(R_{n})|_{(Y^{k_{0}+1})^{3}}\lesssim 1+|R_{n}|_{(X^{k_{0}+2})^{3}}\lesssim 1,

\displaystyle|\tilde{G}_{s}(R_{n})|_{Y^{k_{0}+1+\beta}\times(Y^{k_{0}+\beta})^{2}}\lesssim 1+|R_{n}|_{(X^{k_{0}+2+\beta})^{3}}.

therefore, we have

\displaystyle C_{n}\lesssim\frac{1}{s}(1+|R_{n}|_{(X^{k_{0}+4+\beta})^{3}}).

For
R_{n}
, we have

\displaystyle|R_{n}|_{(X^{k_{0}+4+\beta})^{3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\left|R_{j+1}-R_{j}\right|_{(X^{k_{0}+4+\beta})^{3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\left|S(N_{j})T_{s}(R_{j})^{-1}[\tilde{G}_{s}(R_{j})]\right|_{(X^{k_{0}+4+\beta})^{3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}N_{j}^{3}C_{j}

\displaystyle\lesssim n\sup_{j=0,\ldots,n-1}N_{j}^{3}C_{j}.

Hence, we obtain the desired result.
∎

Now we are ready to prove the main theorem of this section.

Proof of Theorem
4.7
:

We fix
k_{0}\geq 2
and pick
\tilde{\epsilon},\ \tilde{s}>0
so that

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}s^{-1+2\left(\frac{67}{64}\right)^{k}}\leq s^{1+\tilde{\epsilon}}\text{ for all $s\in(0,\tilde{s})$.}

(4.77)

And we choose

\displaystyle\ \beta:=816\quad\text{ and }\quad\epsilon:=\min\left\{\frac{1}{4},\frac{\tilde{\epsilon}}{2}\right\}>0,

(4.78)

and let
s_{0}=s_{0}(\epsilon,k_{0}),

\delta=\delta(\epsilon,k_{0})
be as in Lemma
4.9
. As before, we can also assume
\delta
is small enough so that
\delta<\eta(k_{0})
where
\eta
is as in (b’) in Lemma
4.10
. Since
k_{0}
,
\epsilon
and
\beta
are fixed, by Lemma
4.11
and
4.12
, we can find a constant
K>0
such that as long as
R_{n}
satisfies (
4.22
),(
4.23
) and (
4.24
), for some
s\in(0,s_{0})
, it holds that for any sequence of positive numbers
N_{n}
, and

\displaystyle a_{0}\leq Ks

(4.79)

\displaystyle|PR_{1}|_{(X^{k_{0}+2})^{3}}\leq KN_{0}s

(4.80)

\displaystyle|(I-P)R_{1}|_{(X^{k_{0}+2})^{3}}\leq KN_{0}s^{2}.

(4.81)

\displaystyle a_{n}\leq\frac{K}{s}N_{n}b_{n}\quad\text{for $n\geq 1$,}

(4.82)

\displaystyle b_{0}\leq Ks^{2},

(4.83)

\displaystyle b_{1}\leq K\left((N_{0}^{3}+1)s^{3}+N_{0}^{-\beta}C_{0}\right)

(4.84)

\displaystyle b_{n+1}\leq K(a_{n}^{2}+\frac{1}{s}N_{n}b_{n}^{2}+N_{n}^{-\beta}(1+b_{n})C_{n})\quad\text{ for $n\geq 1$},

(4.85)

\displaystyle a^{\prime}_{n}\leq\frac{K}{s}N_{n}^{2}b_{n}\quad\text{ for $n\geq 0$},

(4.86)

\displaystyle a^{\prime\prime}_{n}\leq\frac{K}{s}N_{n}^{4}b_{n}\quad\text{ for $n\geq 0$},

(4.87)

\displaystyle C_{n}\leq\frac{K}{s}\left(1+n\sup_{j=0,\ldots n-1}\left|N_{j}^{4}C_{j}\right|\right)\quad\text{ for $n\geq 1$,}

(4.88)

\displaystyle C_{0}\leq Ks.

(4.89)

In what follows, we assume without loss of generality that

\displaystyle K>1,\quad\text{ and }\quad\delta<1.

(4.90)

For such
K
, we can find
s^{*}
such that for all
s\in(0,s^{*})
(each of them can be easily verified for small enough
s>0
),

\displaystyle 2Kn<s^{-2\left(\frac{67}{64}\right)^{n-1}+1}\quad\text{ for all $n\geq 1$},

(4.91)

\displaystyle 4K^{3}s^{\frac{7}{16}}<\frac{1}{2},

(4.92)

\displaystyle Ks^{\frac{\tilde{\epsilon}}{2}}\leq\frac{1}{2},

(4.93)

\displaystyle s<1,

(4.94)

\displaystyle K^{2}s^{\frac{3}{4}}\leq\frac{1}{2}\delta,

(4.95)

\displaystyle K\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}s^{-1+\frac{127}{64}\left(\frac{67}{64}\right)^{k}}\leq\frac{1}{2}\delta,

(4.96)

\displaystyle K(n+2)\leq s^{1-2\left(\frac{67}{64}\right)^{n}}\text{ for all $n\geq 0$},

(4.97)

\displaystyle 3K^{3}\leq s^{2+\left(-\frac{139}{32}+\frac{143}{64}\cdot\frac{67}{64}\right)\left(\frac{67}{64}\right)^{n}}\text{ for all $n\geq 1$},

(4.98)

\displaystyle 3K\leq s^{1+\left(-\frac{141}{32}+\frac{143}{64}\left(\frac{67}{64}\right)\right)\left(\frac{67}{64}\right)^{n}}\text{ for all $n\geq 1$},

(4.99)

\displaystyle 6K\leq s^{\left(-\frac{816}{16}+\frac{768}{16}+\frac{143}{64}\frac{67}{64}\right)\left(\frac{67}{64}\right)^{n}}\text{ for all $n\geq 1$}.

(4.100)

Lastly, we fix
s\in(0,\min\left\{s_{0},s^{*},\tilde{s}\right\})
and

\displaystyle N_{n}:=s^{-\frac{1}{16}\left(\frac{67}{64}\right)^{n}}\quad\text{ for $n\geq 0$.}

(4.101)

We claim that for all
n\geq 0
,

(P1)_{n}

:
R_{n+1}
satisfies (
4.22
),(
4.23
) and (
4.24
).

(P2)_{n}

:
C_{n}\leq N_{n}^{768}
.

(P3)_{n}

:
b_{n+1}\leq s^{\frac{143}{64}\left(\frac{67}{64}\right)^{n+1}}
.

(P4)_{n}

:
a_{n+1}\leq Ks^{-1+\frac{139}{64}\left(\frac{67}{64}\right)^{n+1}}
.

Once we have the above claims, then
(P1)_{n}
justifies all the recurrence formulae above for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, which follows from Lemma
4.11
and
4.12
. Also, it is clear from (
4.82
) and
(P4)_{n}
that

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}a_{n}\leq KN_{0}s+K\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}s^{-1+\frac{139}{64}\left(\frac{67}{64}\right)^{n}}<\infty.

Therefore
R_{n}
is a Cauchy sequence in
(X^{k_{0}+1})^{3}
, and we can find a limit
R_{\infty}:=\lim_{n\to\infty}R_{n}
. Then,
(P3)_{n}
and the continuity of the functional
R\mapsto\tilde{G}_{s}
implies that
\tilde{G}_{s}(R_{\infty})=0
. From the definition of
\tilde{G}_{s}
in (
4.19
), this implies the existence of a solution
R=R(s)\neq 0
for each
s\in(0,\min\left\{s_{0},s^{*},\tilde{s}\right\})
and finishes the proof.

Now we prove the claim
(Pi)_{n}
for
i=1,\ldots,4
. We will follow the usual induction argument.

Initial step.
In the initial case, we assume that
n=0
. We first prove
(P1)_{0}
. It follows from (
4.80
),(
4.81
) and (
4.101
) that

\displaystyle|PR_{1}|_{(X^{k_{0}+2})^{3}}\leq Ks^{\frac{15}{16}}\leq s^{\frac{1}{4}}\leq s^{\epsilon}

(4.102)

\displaystyle|(I-P)R_{1}|_{(X^{k_{0}+2})^{3}}\leq Ks^{1+\frac{15}{16}}\leq s^{1+\frac{1}{4}}\leq s^{1+\epsilon},

(4.103)

where the two second inequalities follow from (
4.92
) (
Ks^{\frac{15}{16}}\leq 4K^{3}s^{\frac{7}{16}}s^{\frac{1}{2}}<s^{\frac{1}{4}}
) and the last inequalities follow from (
4.94
) and (
4.78
). Furthermore, it follows from (
4.87
), (
4.83
) and (
4.101
) that

\displaystyle|R_{1}|_{(X^{k_{0}+4})^{3}}=a^{\prime\prime}_{0}\leq\frac{K}{s}\cdot\left(s^{-\frac{1}{16}}\right)^{4}\cdot\left(Ks^{2}\right)\leq K^{2}s^{\frac{3}{4}}\leq\frac{1}{2}\delta,

(4.104)

where the last inequality follows from (
4.95
). Therefore
(P1)_{0}
holds for
n=0
.

(P2)_{0}
follows immediately from (
4.89
) and (
4.101
). In order to prove
(P3)_{0}
, note that thanks to (
4.84
), it is enough to show that

K\left((N_{0}^{3}+1)s^{3}+N_{0}^{-\beta}C_{0}\right)\leq s^{\frac{143}{64}\cdot\frac{67}{64}},

in other words,

K\left(s^{\frac{45}{16}}+s^{3}+Ks^{\frac{816}{16}}s\right)\leq s^{\frac{143}{64}\cdot\frac{67}{64}},

where we used (
4.78
), (
4.89
) and (
4.101
). By (
4.94
),
s^{\frac{45}{16}}
is the largest value among the three in the parentheses, hence it is sufficient to show that
3K^{2}<s^{\frac{143}{64}\cdot\frac{67}{64}-\frac{45}{16}}.
Since
\frac{143}{64}\cdot\frac{67}{64}-\frac{45}{16}>-\frac{1}{2}
, it is enough to show that
3K^{3}\leq s^{-\frac{1}{2}}
, which follows from (
4.92
).
(P4)_{0}
follows from (
4.82
), (
4.101
) and
(P3)_{0}
.

Induction step.

In this step, we assume that
(Pi)_{k}
is true for all
0\leq k\leq n_{0}
and aim to prove
(Pi)_{n_{0}+1}
. Let us prove
(P1)_{n_{0}+1}
first. It follows from (
4.86
), (
4.101
) and
(P3)_{k}
for
k=0,\ldots,n_{0}
, that

\sum_{k=1}^{n_{0}+1}a^{\prime}_{k}\leq K\sum_{k=1}^{n_{0}+1}s^{-1}\left(s^{-\frac{1}{16}\left(\frac{67}{64}\right)^{k}}\right)^{2}\cdot s^{\frac{143}{64}\left(\frac{67}{64}\right)^{k}}\leq K\sum_{k=1}^{n_{0}+1}s^{-1+2\left(\frac{67}{64}\right)^{k}}\leq Ks^{1+\tilde{\epsilon}},

where the last inequality follows from our choice on
\tilde{\epsilon}
and
s\in\tilde{s}
in (
4.77
). Hence, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n_{0}+1}a^{\prime}_{k}\leq\left(Ks^{\frac{\tilde{\epsilon}}{2}}\right)s^{1+\frac{\tilde{\epsilon}}{2}}\leq\frac{1}{2}s^{1+\frac{\tilde{\epsilon}}{2}}\leq\frac{1}{2}s^{1+\epsilon},

where the second inequality follows from (
4.93
) and the last inequality follows from (
4.78
) and (
4.94
). Therefore we obtain

|PR_{n_{0}+2}|_{(X^{k_{0}+2})^{3}}\leq|PR_{1}|_{(X^{k_{0}+2})^{3}}+\sum_{k=1}^{n_{0}+1}a^{\prime}_{k}\leq Ks^{\frac{15}{16}}+\frac{1}{2}s^{1+\epsilon}\leq\left(Ks^{\frac{7}{16}}\right)s^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left(\frac{1}{2}s\right)s^{\epsilon}\leq s^{\epsilon},

where the second inequality follows from (
4.102
) and the last inequality follows from
\epsilon<\frac{1}{2}
and
Ks^{\frac{7}{16}}\leq\frac{1}{2}
, which can be deduced from (
4.93
) and (
4.78
). Using (
4.103
), instead of (
4.102
), one can easily obtain

|(I-P)R_{n_{0}+2}|_{(X^{k_{0}+2})^{3}}\leq s^{1+\epsilon}.

To prove (
4.24
) for
R_{n_{0}+2}
, we compute

\displaystyle|R_{n_{0}+2}|_{(H^{k_{0}+4})^{3}}

\displaystyle\leq a^{\prime\prime}_{0}+\sum_{k=1}^{n_{0}+1}a^{\prime\prime}_{k}\leq\frac{1}{2}\delta+K\sum_{k=1}^{n_{0}+1}s^{-1}\left(s^{-\frac{1}{16}\left(\frac{67}{64}\right)^{k}}\right)^{4}\cdot s^{\frac{143}{64}\left(\frac{67}{64}\right)^{k}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\delta+K\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}s^{-1+\frac{127}{64}\left(\frac{67}{64}\right)^{k}}\leq\delta,

(4.105)

where the second inequality follows from (
4.104
), (
4.87
) and
(P3)_{k}
, for
0\leq k\leq n_{0}
, and the last inequality follows from (
4.96
). This proves
(P1)_{n_{0}+1}
.

We turn to
(P2)_{n_{0}+1}
. Using (
4.88
) and
(P2)_{n_{0}}
, we have

C_{n_{0}+1}\leq\frac{K}{s}(2+n_{0})N_{n_{0}}^{4}\sup_{j=0,\ldots,n_{0}}C_{j}\leq\frac{K}{s}(2+n_{0})N_{n_{0}}^{772},

where the last inequality follows from
(P2)_{n_{0}}
. Hence, it suffices to show that

\frac{K}{s}(2+n_{0})N_{n_{0}}^{772}\leq(N_{n_{0}+1})^{768}.

Plugging (
4.101
), this is equivalent to

K(n_{0}+2)\leq s^{1+\left(\frac{67}{64}\right)^{n_{0}}\left(\frac{772}{16}-\frac{768}{16}\cdot\frac{67}{64}\right)}=s^{1-2\left(\frac{67}{64}\right)^{n_{0}}},

which is true thanks to our choice of
s
in (
4.97
). This proves
(P2)_{n_{0}+1}
.

For
(P3)_{n_{0}+1}
, thanks to (
4.85
), it is enough to show that

K\left(a_{n_{0}+1}^{2}+\frac{1}{s}N_{n_{0}+1}b_{n_{0}+1}^{2}+N_{n_{0}+1}^{-\beta}(1+b_{n_{0}+1})C_{n_{0}+1})\right)\leq s^{\frac{143}{64}\left(\frac{67}{64}\right)^{n_{0}+2}}.

Using
(P4)_{n_{0}}
, (
4.101
),
(P3)_{n_{0}}
, (
4.78
),
(P2)_{n_{0}+1}
and (
4.94
), which implies
b_{n_{0}+1}\leq 1
, we only need to show that

\displaystyle K\left(\underbrace{\left(Ks^{-1+\frac{139}{64}\left(\frac{67}{64}\right)^{n_{0}+1}}\right)^{2}}_{=:A_{1}}+\underbrace{s^{-1}s^{-\frac{1}{16}\left(\frac{67}{64}\right)^{n_{0}+1}}s^{\frac{143}{32}\left(\frac{67}{64}\right)^{n_{0}+1}}}_{=:A_{2}}+\underbrace{2s^{\frac{816}{16}\left(\frac{67}{64}\right)^{n_{0}+1}}s^{-\frac{768}{16}\left(\frac{67}{64}\right)^{n_{0}+1}}}_{=:A_{3}}\right)\leq s^{\frac{143}{64}\left(\frac{67}{64}\right)^{n_{0}+2}}.

(4.106)

We will show that
KA_{i}\leq\frac{1}{3}s^{\frac{143}{64}\left(\frac{67}{64}\right)^{n_{0}+2}}
for
i=1,2,3
. For
A_{1}
, it suffices to show that

K^{3}s^{-2+\frac{139}{32}\left(\frac{67}{64}\right)^{n_{0}+1}}\leq\frac{1}{3}s^{\frac{143}{64}\left(\frac{67}{64}\right)^{n_{0}+2}},

equivalently,

3K^{3}\leq s^{2+\left(-\frac{139}{32}+\frac{143}{64}\cdot\frac{67}{64}\right)\left(\frac{67}{64}\right)^{n_{0}+1}},

and this follows from our choice of
s
in (
4.98
). For
A_{2}
, we need to show that

Ks^{-1+\frac{141}{32}\left(\frac{67}{64}\right)^{n_{0}+1}}\leq\frac{1}{3}s^{\frac{143}{64}\left(\frac{67}{64}\right)^{n_{0}+2}},

eqvalently,

3K\leq s^{1+\left(-\frac{141}{32}+\frac{143}{64}\left(\frac{67}{64}\right)\right)\left(\frac{67}{64}\right)^{n_{0}+1}},

and this follows from (
4.99
). For
A_{3}
, it is enough to show that

2Ks^{\frac{816}{16}\left(\frac{67}{64}\right)^{n_{0}+1}}\cdot s^{-\frac{768}{16}\left(\frac{67}{64}\right)^{n_{0}+1}}\leq\frac{1}{3}s^{\frac{143}{64}\left(\frac{67}{64}\right)^{n_{0}+2}},

equivalently,

6K\leq s^{\left(-\frac{816}{16}+\frac{768}{16}+\frac{143}{64}\frac{67}{64}\right)\left(\frac{67}{64}\right)^{n_{0}+1}},

which follows from (
4.100
). This proves
(P3)_{n_{0}+1}
.

Lastly,
(P4)_{n_{0}+1}
can be proved by (
4.82
) and
(P3)_{n_{0}+1}
, that is,

a_{n_{0}+2}\leq Ks^{-1}N_{n_{0}+2}b_{n_{0}+2}\leq Ks^{-1}s^{\left(-\frac{1}{16}+\frac{143}{64}\right)\left(\frac{67}{64}\right)^{n_{0}+2}}=Ks^{-1+\frac{139}{64}\left(\frac{67}{64}\right)^{n_{0}+2}},

which finishes the proof.

\Box

4.4
Estimates on the velocity

In Subsection
4.5
, we will check whether our functional
G
in (
4.5
) satisfies the hypotheses in Theorem
4.7
. In this section, we will derive some useful estimates on the velocity vector generated by each patch. Recall that
given
b_{1},b_{2}>0
,
b_{1}\neq b_{2}
, and
R_{1},R_{2}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})
, we denote for
i,j=1,2
,

\displaystyle R=(R_{1},R_{2})\in(C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}))^{2},

(4.107)

\displaystyle z_{i}(R)(x)=(b_{i}+R_{i}(x))(\cos x,\sin x),

(4.108)

\displaystyle u_{i,j}(R)(x):=\begin{pmatrix}u^{1}_{i,j}(R)\\
u^{2}_{i,j}(R)\end{pmatrix}=\int_{\mathbb{T}}\log(|z_{j}(R)(x)-z_{i}(R)(y)|^{2})z_{i}(R)^{\prime\perp}(y)dy.

(4.109)

Proposition 4.13

For
k\geq 2
, there exists
\epsilon=\epsilon(k,b_{1},b_{2})>0
such that if
\rVert R\rVert_{(H^{3}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\leq\epsilon
, then

(A)

u_{i,j}:(H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}\mapsto(H^{k}(\mathbb{T}))^{2},\text{ and }\rVert u_{i,j}\rVert_{(H^{k}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\lesssim_{k,b_{1},b_{2}}1+\rVert R\rVert_{(H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}.

(B)

The Gateaux derivative
Du_{i,j}(R):(H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}\mapsto(H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}
exists and

\rVert Du_{i,j}(R)[h]\rVert_{(H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\lesssim_{k,b_{1},b_{2}}\rVert h\rVert_{(H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}+\rVert R\rVert_{(H^{k+3}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\rVert h\rVert_{(H^{1}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}},\text{ for }h\in(C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}.

(C)

The map
R\mapsto Du_{{i,j}}(R)
is Lipschitz continuous, in the sense that for
R,r,h\in(C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}
such that
\rVert R\rVert_{(H^{3}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}},\rVert r\rVert_{(H^{3}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\leq\epsilon,
and
\rVert R\rVert_{(H^{k+3}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}},\rVert r\rVert_{(H^{k+3}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\leq 1
,

\rVert Du_{i,j}(R)[h]-Du_{i,j}(r)[h]\rVert_{(H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\lesssim_{k,b_{1},b_{2}}\rVert R-r\rVert_{(H^{2}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\rVert h\rVert_{(H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}+\rVert R-r\rVert_{(H^{k+3}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\rVert h\rVert_{(H^{1}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}.

(D)

For
\sigma\in\mathbb{N}\cup\left\{0\right\}
, there exists a linear operator
T_{i,j}^{\sigma}(R)
such that for
h\in\left(C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})\right)^{2}
,

\left(\frac{d}{dx}\right)^{\sigma}\left(Du_{i,j}(R)[h]\right)=Du_{i,j}(R)[h^{(\sigma)}]+T_{i,j}^{\sigma}(R)[h],

where
T_{i,j}^{\sigma}(R)
satisfies

\rVert T_{i,j}^{\sigma}(R)[h]\rVert_{(H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\lesssim_{k}(1+\rVert R\rVert_{(H^{k+4+\sigma}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}})\rVert h\rVert_{(L^{2}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}+(1+\rVert R\rVert_{(H^{4}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}})\rVert h^{(k+\sigma)}\rVert_{(L^{2}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}.

Remark 4.14

It is well known that roughly speaking, if
\partial D
is
C^{k+\alpha}
-regular for some
k\geq 1,

\alpha>0
then the velocity
\nabla^{\perp}(1_{D}*\log|x|)
is also
C^{k+\alpha}
-regular up to the boundary. In
(A)
in the above Proposition, we do not aim to prove the optimal regularity since it is not necessary in the proof of the main theorem.

The proof of the proposition will be given after Lemmas
4.15
and
4.16
. We will deal with the case
i=j
only. If
i\neq j
, then the integrand in
u_{i,j}
has no singularity thus the result follows straightforwardly in a similar manner. Hence, by abuse of notation, we denote for
b>0
and
R\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})
,

\displaystyle z(R)(x):=(b+R(x))(\cos x,\sin x),

(4.110)

\displaystyle u(R)(x):=\begin{pmatrix}u^{1}(R)\\
u^{2}(R)\end{pmatrix}:=\int_{\mathbb{T}}\log\left(|z(R)(x)-z(R)(y)|^{2}\right)z(R)^{\prime\perp}(y)dy.

(4.111)

Let us write
u(R)
as

\displaystyle u(R)(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}}\log\left(2-2\cos(x-y)\right)z(R)^{\prime\perp}(y)dy+\int_{\mathbb{T}}\log\left(\frac{|z(R)(x)-z(R)(y)|^{2}}{2-2\cos(x-y)}\right)z(R)^{\prime\perp}(y)dy

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}}\log\left(2-2\cos(x-y)\right)z(R)^{\prime\perp}(y)dy+\int_{\mathbb{T}}K(R)(x,y)z(R)^{\prime}(y)^{\perp}dy

\displaystyle=:u_{L}(R)(x)+u_{N}(R)(x),

(4.112)

where

\displaystyle K(R)(x,y)=F(R(x),R(y),J(R)(x,y)),

\displaystyle F(u,v,w):=\log(b^{2}+b(u+v)+uv+w^{2}),

(4.113)

\displaystyle J(R)(x,y)=\frac{R(x)-R(y)}{2\sin(\frac{x-y}{2})}.

If
\rVert R\rVert_{H^{3}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\epsilon
for sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
, it follows from Lemmas
A.10
and
A.5
that

\displaystyle\rVert J(R)\rVert_{H^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}\lesssim_{b}\rVert R\rVert_{H^{3}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim\epsilon.

(4.114)

Therefore, for sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
, Lemmas
A.8
and
A.10
imply that for
l\geq 2
,

\displaystyle\rVert K(R)\rVert_{H^{l}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}\lesssim_{l,b}1+\rVert R\rVert_{H^{l}(\mathbb{T})}+\rVert J(R)\rVert_{H^{l}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}\lesssim_{l,b}1+\rVert R\rVert_{H^{l+1}(\mathbb{T})},,

(4.115)

The next lemma will be used to prove the growth condition of the velocity in (A) in Proposition
4.13
.

Lemma 4.15

For
k\geq 2
, there exists
\epsilon=\epsilon(k,b)>0
such that if
R\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})
and
\rVert R\rVert_{H^{3}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\epsilon
, then

\rVert u\rVert_{(H^{k}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\lesssim_{k,b}1+\rVert R\rVert_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})}.

Proof.

For
u_{L}
, then it follows from Lemma
A.14
and the fact that
R\mapsto u_{L}(R)
is linear that

\rVert u_{L}(R)\rVert_{(H^{k}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\lesssim\rVert z(R)^{\prime}\rVert_{(H^{k-1}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\lesssim 1+\rVert R\rVert_{H^{k}(\mathbb{T})}.

Now, let us consider the nonlinear part. We have

\rVert u_{N}\rVert_{(H^{k}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\lesssim\rVert K(R)\rVert_{H^{k}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}\rVert z(R)^{\prime}\rVert_{(L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\lesssim 1+\rVert R\rVert_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})},

where the last inequality follows from (
4.115
).
∎

Now we turn to (B), (C) and (D) in Proposition
4.13
. We will use the following notations:

\displaystyle DK(R)[h]:=\frac{d}{dt}K(R+th)|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\partial_{u}F(R(x),R(y),J(R)(x,y))}_{=:\partial_{u}F(R)(x,y)}h(x)

\displaystyle\ +\underbrace{\partial_{v}F(R(x),R(y),J(R)(x,y))}_{=:\partial_{v}F(R)(x,y)}h(y)

\displaystyle\ +\underbrace{\partial_{w}F(R(x),R(y),J(R)(x,y))}_{=:\partial_{w}F(R)(x,y)}J(h)(x,y),

(4.116)

where
J(h):=\frac{h(x)-h(y)}{2\sin\left(\frac{x-y}{2}\right)}
and

\displaystyle Dz(R)^{\prime\perp}[h]:=\frac{d}{dt}z(R+th)^{\prime\perp}|_{t=0}=-h^{\prime}(x)(\sin x,\cos x)-h(x)(\cos x,\sin x).

(4.117)

With the above notations, we can write the derivative of the nonlinear term as

\displaystyle Du_{N}(R)[h]

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}}K(R)(x,y)Dz(R)^{\prime\perp}[h](y)dy+\int_{\mathbb{T}}DK(R)[h](x,y)z(R)^{\prime\perp}(y)dy

\displaystyle=:Du_{N}^{1}(R)[h]+Du_{N}^{2}(R)[h].

(4.118)

Again, Lemma
A.8
and (
4.114
) yield that if
\rVert R\rVert_{H^{3}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\epsilon
for sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
, then for
l\geq 2
,

\displaystyle\rVert\partial_{u}F(R)\rVert_{(H^{l}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}},\rVert\partial_{v}F(R)\rVert_{(H^{l}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}},\rVert\partial_{w}F(R)\rVert_{(H^{l}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{l}1+\rVert R\rVert_{H^{l}(\mathbb{T})}+\rVert J(R)\rVert_{H^{l}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{l}1+\rVert R\rVert_{H^{l+1}(\mathbb{T})}.

(4.119)

Also, if
R_{1},R_{2}\in C^{\infty}({\mathbb{T}})
and
\rVert R_{1}\rVert_{H^{3}(\mathbb{T})},\rVert R_{2}\rVert_{H^{3}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\epsilon
, then from Lemma
A.8
, it follows that

\displaystyle\rVert\partial_{u}F(R_{1})-\partial_{u}F(R_{2})\rVert_{(H^{l}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}},\rVert\partial_{v}F(R_{1})-\partial_{v}F(R_{2})\rVert_{(H^{l}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}},\rVert\partial_{w}F(R_{1})-\partial_{w}F(R_{2})\rVert_{(H^{l}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\lesssim_{l}\rVert R_{1}-R_{2}\rVert_{H^{l+1}(\mathbb{T})}.

(4.120)

For
{\partial_{w}F^{\#}(R)}(x):=\lim_{y\to x}\partial_{w}F(R)(x,y)=\partial_{w}F(R(x),R(x),R^{\prime}(x))
, we have

\displaystyle\rVert{\partial_{w}F^{\#}(R)}\rVert_{H^{l}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim_{l}1+\rVert R\rVert_{H^{l+1}(\mathbb{T})},

(4.121)

\displaystyle\rVert{\partial_{w}F^{\#}(R)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim 1+\rVert R\rVert_{H^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim 1.

(4.122)

\displaystyle\rVert{\partial_{w}F^{\#}(R_{1})}-{\partial_{w}F^{\#}(R_{2})}\rVert_{H^{l}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim_{l}\rVert R_{1}-R_{2}\rVert_{H^{l+1}(\mathbb{T})},

(4.123)

\displaystyle\rVert{\partial_{w}F^{\#}(R_{1})}-{\partial_{w}F^{\#}(R_{2})}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim\rVert R_{1}-R_{2}\rVert_{H^{2}(\mathbb{T})}.

(4.124)

We decompose
Du_{N}^{2}(R)[h]
into

\displaystyle Du_{N}^{2}(R)[h]

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}}\underbrace{\partial_{u}F(R)(x,y)z(R)^{\prime\perp}}_{=:K_{1}(R)(x,y)}h(x)dy+\int_{\mathbb{T}}\underbrace{\partial_{v}F(R)(x,y)z(R)^{\prime\perp}}_{=:K_{2}(R)(x,y)}h(y)dy

\displaystyle\ +\int_{\mathbb{T}}\underbrace{\partial_{w}F(R)(x,y)z(R)^{\prime\perp}}_{=:K_{3}(R)(x,y)}J(h)(x,y)dy

(4.125)

\displaystyle=:Du_{N1}^{2}(R)[h]+Du_{N2}^{2}(R)[h]+Du_{N3}^{2}(R)[h].

(4.126)

Then it follows from Lemma
A.5
, (
4.4
), (
4.121
) and (
4.122
) that for
l\geq 2
,

\displaystyle\rVert K_{i}(R)\rVert_{H^{l}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}\lesssim_{l}1+\rVert R\rVert_{H^{l+1}(\mathbb{T})},\text{ for }i=1,2,3,

(4.127)

\displaystyle\rVert{K_{3}}^{\#}(R)\rVert_{H^{l}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim 1+\rVert R\rVert_{H^{l+1}(\mathbb{T})},\text{ where }{K_{3}}^{\#}(R)(x):=K_{3}(R)(x,x),

(4.128)

\displaystyle\rVert{K_{3}}^{\#}(R)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim\rVert{K_{3}}^{\#}(R)\rVert_{H^{1}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim\rVert{K_{3}}^{\#}(R)\rVert_{H^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim 1+\rVert R\rVert_{H^{3}({\mathbb{T}})}\lesssim 1,

(4.129)

\displaystyle\rVert K_{i}(R_{1})-K_{2}(R_{2})\rVert_{H^{l}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}\lesssim_{l}\rVert R_{1}-R_{2}\rVert_{H^{l+1}(\mathbb{T})},\text{ for }i=1,2,3,

(4.130)

\displaystyle\rVert{K_{3}}^{\#}(R_{1})-{K_{3}}^{\#}(R_{2})\rVert_{H^{l}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim\rVert R_{1}-R_{2}\rVert_{H^{l+1}(\mathbb{T})},

(4.131)

\displaystyle\rVert{K_{3}}^{\#}(R_{1})-{K_{3}}^{\#}(R_{2})\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim\rVert R_{1}-R_{2}\rVert_{H^{2}(\mathbb{T})}.

(4.132)

Furthermore, it follows from the definitions of
K_{3}
and
\partial_{w}F
in (
4.125
), (
4.116
) and (
4.113
) that

\displaystyle\rVert\nabla K_{3}(R)(x,y)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}\lesssim 1+\rVert R^{\prime\prime}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}+\rVert\nabla J(R)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}\lesssim 1+\rVert R^{\prime\prime}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim 1.

(4.133)

Lemma 4.16

For
k\geq 2
, there exists
\epsilon=\epsilon(k,b)>0
such that if
R\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})
and
\rVert R\rVert_{H^{3}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\epsilon
, the Gateaux derivative
Du(R):H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})\mapsto(H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}
exists and

\displaystyle\rVert Du(R)[h]\rVert_{(H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\lesssim_{k,b}\rVert h\rVert_{(H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}+\rVert R\rVert_{H^{k+3}(\mathbb{T})}\rVert h\rVert_{H^{1}(\mathbb{T})},\text{ for }h\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}).

(4.134)

Furthermore, if
R_{1},R_{2}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})
,
\rVert R_{1}\rVert_{H^{3}(\mathbb{T})},\rVert R_{2}\rVert_{H^{3}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\epsilon
, and
\rVert R_{1}\rVert_{H^{k+3}(\mathbb{T})},\rVert R_{2}\rVert_{H^{k+3}(\mathbb{T})}\leq 1
, then

\displaystyle\rVert Du(R_{1})-Du(R_{2})[h]\rVert_{(H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\lesssim_{k,b}\rVert R_{1}-R_{2}\rVert_{H^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\rVert h\rVert_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})}+\rVert R_{1}-R_{2}\rVert_{H^{k+3}(\mathbb{T})}\rVert h\rVert_{H^{1}(\mathbb{T})},

(4.135)

\text{ for }h\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}).

Lastly, for
\sigma\in\mathbb{N}\cup\left\{0\right\}
, there exists a linear operator
T_{\sigma}(R)
such that for
h\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})
,

\displaystyle\left(\frac{d}{dx}\right)^{\sigma}\left(Du(R)[h]\right)=Du(R)[h^{(\sigma)}]+T^{\sigma}(R)[h],

(4.136)

where
T^{\sigma}(R)
satisfies

\displaystyle\rVert T^{\sigma}(R)[h]\rVert_{(H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\lesssim_{k}((1+\rVert R\rVert_{H^{k+4+\sigma}(\mathbb{T})})\rVert h\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}+(1+\rVert R\rVert_{H^{4}(\mathbb{T})})\rVert h^{(k+\sigma)}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}.

(4.137)

Proof.

We omit the proof of (
4.135
) since it can be proved exactly same way as (
4.134
) with (
A.3
) in Lemma
A.8
. In order to prove (
4.134
), we deal with
Du_{L}
. Since it is linear, we have

Du_{L}(R)[h](x):=\int_{\mathbb{T}}\log(2-2\cos(x-y))Dz(R)^{\prime\perp}[h](y)dy.

Hence, it follows from Lemma
A.14
that

\displaystyle\rVert Du_{L}(R)[h]\rVert_{(H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\lesssim\rVert Dz(R)^{\prime\perp}[h]\rVert_{(H^{k}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\lesssim\rVert h\rVert_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})}.

(4.138)

Now, we estimate
Du_{N}
. Recalling the decomposition in (
4.4
) we will estimate
Du_{N}^{1}
and
Du_{N}^{2}
separately. For
Du_{N}^{1}
, it follows from (
4.115
) and (
4.117
) that

\displaystyle\rVert Du_{N}^{1}(R)[h]\rVert_{(H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\lesssim\rVert K(R)\rVert_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}\rVert Dz(R)^{\prime\perp}\rVert_{(L^{2}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\lesssim(1+\rVert R\rVert_{H^{k+2}(\mathbb{T})})\rVert h\rVert_{H^{1}(\mathbb{T})}.

(4.139)

For
Du_{N}^{2}
, we recall the decomposition in (
4.126
), and use (
4.127
) and Lemma
A.11
to obtain

\rVert Du_{N1}^{2}(R)[h]\rVert_{(H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}},\rVert Du_{N2}^{2}(R)[h]\rVert_{(H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\lesssim\rVert h\rVert_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})}+\rVert R\rVert_{H^{k+2}(\mathbb{T})}\rVert h\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}.

For
Du_{N3}^{2}(R)[h]
, we use (
4.127
), (
4.128
), (
4.129
), (
4.133
) and Lemma
A.12
to show that

\rVert Du_{N3}^{2}(R)[h]\rVert_{(H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\lesssim\rVert R\rVert_{H^{k+3}(\mathbb{T})}\rVert h\rVert_{H^{1}(\mathbb{T})}+\rVert h\rVert_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})}.

Therefore
\rVert Du_{N}^{2}(R)[h]\rVert_{(H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\lesssim\rVert h\rVert_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})}+\rVert R\rVert_{H^{k+3}(\mathbb{T})}\rVert h\rVert_{H^{1}(\mathbb{T})}
. With (
4.138
), (
4.139
) and (
4.4
), the desired result follows.

Now, we turn to (
4.136
) and (
4.137
). Recall from (
4.4
), (
4.4
) and (
4.126
) that

\displaystyle Du(R)[h]

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}}\log(2-2\cos(y))h(x-y)dy+\int_{\mathbb{T}}K(R)(x,y)Dz(R)^{\prime\perp}[h](y)dy

\displaystyle\ +\int_{\mathbb{T}}K_{1}(R)(x,y)z(R)^{\prime\perp}(y)h(x)dy+\int_{\mathbb{T}}K_{2}(R)(x,y)z(R)^{\prime\perp}(y)h(y)dy

\displaystyle\ +\int_{\mathbb{T}}K_{3}(R)(x,y)z(R)^{\prime\perp}(y)J(h)(x,y)dy

\displaystyle=I_{1}(R)[h]+I_{2}(R)[h]+I_{3}(R)[h]+I_{4}(R)[h]+I_{5}(R)[h]

where
K,K_{1},K_{2},K_{3}
are of the form
H(R(x),R(y),J(R)(x,y))
for some smooth function
H:\mathbb{R}^{3}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
. It suffices to show that for each
i=1,\ldots,5
and
\sigma\in\mathbb{N}\cup\left\{0\right\}
,

\left(\frac{d}{dx}\right)^{\sigma}I_{i}(R)[h]=I_{i}(R)[h^{\sigma}]+T_{i}^{\sigma}(R)[h],

for some
T_{i}^{\sigma}(R)
such that

\displaystyle\rVert T_{i}^{\sigma}(R)[h]\rVert_{(H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\lesssim(1+\rVert R\rVert_{H^{k+4+\sigma}(\mathbb{T})})\rVert h\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}+(1+\rVert R\rVert_{H^{4}(\mathbb{T})})\rVert h^{(k+\sigma)}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}.

(4.140)

We only deal with
I_{5}
since the other terms can be done in the same way. We also assume
\sigma\geq 1
since
\sigma=0
follows trivially.
Let
\tilde{K}_{3}(R)(x,y):=K_{3}(R)(x,x-y)z(R)^{\prime\perp}(x-y)
. Then it follows from (
A.5
), (
4.127
), (
4.128
) and (
4.129
) that for
l\geq 2
,

\displaystyle\rVert\tilde{K}_{3}(R)\rVert_{H^{l}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}\lesssim_{l}1+\rVert R\rVert_{H^{l+1}(\mathbb{T})},

(4.141)

\displaystyle\rVert{\tilde{K}_{3}}^{\#}(R)\rVert_{H^{l}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim 1+\rVert R\rVert_{H^{l+1}(\mathbb{T})},\text{ where }{\tilde{K}_{3}}^{\#}(R)(x):=\tilde{K}_{3}(R)(x,x),.

(4.142)

Using the change of variables,
y\mapsto x-y
, we have

\displaystyle\left(\frac{d}{dx}\right)^{\sigma}I_{5}(R)[h]

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}}\tilde{K}_{3}(R)(x,y)J(h^{(\sigma)})(x,x-y)dy+\sum_{p+q=\sigma,q\leq\sigma-1}C_{p,q,\sigma}\int_{\mathbb{T}}(\partial_{x})^{p}\tilde{K}_{3}(R)(x,y)J(h^{(q)})(x,x-y)dy,

\displaystyle=:I_{5}(R)[h^{(\sigma)}](x)+\sum_{p+q=\sigma,q\leq\sigma-1}C_{p,q,\sigma}\underbrace{\int_{\mathbb{T}}(\partial_{x})^{p}\tilde{K}_{3}(R)(x,x-y)J(h^{(q)})(x,y)dy}_{=:T_{5}^{\sigma,p,q}(R)[h](x)}

(4.143)

\displaystyle=:I_{5}(R)[h^{(\sigma)}](x)+T_{5}^{\sigma}(R)[h](x),

(4.144)

where
C_{p,q,\sigma}
is some constant and we used
\partial_{x}\left(J(h)(x,x-y)\right)=J(h^{\prime})(x,x-y)
. It suffices to show that
T_{5}^{\sigma,p,q}(R)[h]
satisfies (
4.140
). Let
\tilde{K}_{3}^{*}(R)(x,y):=(\partial_{x})^{p}\tilde{K}_{3}(R)(x,x-y)
.
Then it follows from (
4.141
) and (
4.142
) that for
l\geq 2
,

\displaystyle\rVert\tilde{K}^{*}_{3}(R)\rVert_{(H^{l}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))^{2}}\lesssim_{l}1+\rVert R\rVert_{H^{l+p+1}(\mathbb{T})},

(4.145)

\displaystyle\rVert({\tilde{K}^{*}_{3}})^{\#}(R)\rVert_{(H^{l}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\lesssim 1+\rVert R\rVert_{H^{l+p+1}(\mathbb{T})},\text{ where }({\tilde{K}^{*}_{3}})^{\#}(R)(x):=\tilde{K}^{*}_{3}(R)(x,x),

(4.146)

\displaystyle\rVert({\tilde{K}^{*}_{3}})^{\#}(R)\rVert_{(L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\lesssim\rVert\tilde{K}_{3}(R)\rVert_{(W^{p,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))^{2}}\lesssim\rVert\tilde{K}_{3}(R)\rVert_{(H^{p+2}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))^{2}}.

(4.147)

Furthermore, it follows similarly as (
4.133
) that

\displaystyle\rVert\nabla\tilde{K}^{*}_{3}(R)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}\lesssim\rVert\nabla^{(p+1)}\tilde{K}_{3}(R)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}\lesssim 1+\rVert R^{(p+2)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim 1+\rVert R\rVert_{H^{p+3}(\mathbb{T})}.

(4.148)

Hence it follows from Lemma
A.12
that

\displaystyle\rVert T^{\sigma,p,q}_{5}(R)[h]\rVert_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\rVert\tilde{K}^{*}_{3}(R)\rVert_{(H^{k+2}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))^{2}}+\rVert({\tilde{K}^{*}_{3}})^{\#}(R)\rVert_{(H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))^{2}}\right)\rVert h^{(q)}\rVert_{H^{1}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle+\left(\rVert({\tilde{K}^{*}_{3}})^{\#}(R)\rVert_{(L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))^{2}}+\rVert\nabla\tilde{K}^{*}_{3}(R)\rVert_{(L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))^{2}}\right)\rVert h^{(q)}\rVert_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+\rVert R\rVert_{H^{k+3+p}(\mathbb{T})})\rVert h^{(q)}\rVert_{H^{1}(\mathbb{T})}+(1+\rVert R\rVert_{H^{p+3}(\mathbb{T})})\rVert h^{(q)}\rVert_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+\rVert R\rVert_{H^{k+3+p}(\mathbb{T})})\rVert h\rVert_{H^{1+q}(\mathbb{T})}+(1+\rVert R\rVert_{H^{p+3}(\mathbb{T})})\rVert h\rVert_{H^{k+1+q}(\mathbb{T})}

where the second last inequality follows from (
4.148
).
Now, recalling that
\rVert R\rVert_{H^{3}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\epsilon
and plugging the following inequalities into the above estimate,

\rVert R\rVert_{H^{l}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim\rVert R\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}+\rVert R^{(l)}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})},\quad\rVert h\rVert_{H^{l}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim\rVert h\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}+\rVert h^{(l)}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})},\text{ for }l\geq 0,

we obtain

\displaystyle\rVert T^{\sigma,p,q}_{5}(R)[h]\rVert_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+\rVert R^{(k+p+3)}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})})\left(\rVert h\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}+\rVert h^{(1+q)}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\right)

\displaystyle\ +(1+\rVert R^{(p+3)}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})})(\rVert h\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}+\rVert h^{(k+1+q)}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})})

\displaystyle=:L_{1}+L_{2}.

(4.149)

For
L_{1}
, note that
k+p+3\leq k+3+\sigma
and
q\leq\sigma-1
, hence we have

\displaystyle L_{1}\lesssim(1+\rVert R^{(k+\sigma+3)}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})})\rVert h\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}+\rVert h^{(\sigma)}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}+\rVert R^{(k+p+3)}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\rVert h^{(1+q)}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}.

(4.150)

Using the interpolation inequality in Lemma
A.6
, we have

\displaystyle\rVert R^{(k+p+3)}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}=\rVert(R^{(4)})^{(k+p-1)}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim\rVert R^{(4)}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}^{1-\frac{k+p-1}{k+\sigma}}\rVert(R^{(4)})^{(k+\sigma)}\rVert_{L^{2}({\mathbb{T})}}^{\frac{k+p-1}{k+\sigma}}

\displaystyle\rVert h^{(1+q)}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim\rVert h\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}^{1-\frac{1+q}{k+\sigma}}\rVert h^{(k+\sigma)}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}^{\frac{1+q}{k+\sigma}}.

Thus, we have

\displaystyle\rVert R^{(k+p+3)}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\rVert h^{(1+q)}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\rVert R^{(4)}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\rVert h^{(k+\sigma)}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\right)^{\frac{1+q}{k+\sigma}}\left(\rVert(R^{(4)})^{(k+\sigma)}\rVert_{L^{2}({\mathbb{T})}}\rVert h\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\right)^{\frac{k+p-1}{k+\sigma}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\rVert R^{(4)}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\rVert h^{(k+\sigma)}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}+\rVert R^{(k+4+\sigma)}\rVert_{L^{2}({\mathbb{T})}}\rVert h\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})},

where we used
p+q=\sigma
and Young’s inequality. Plugging this inequality into (
4.150
) and using
\rVert h^{(\sigma)}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim\rVert h\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}+\rVert h^{(k+\sigma)}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}
to estimate the second term on the right-hand side in (
4.150
), we obtain

L_{1}\lesssim(1+\rVert R\rVert_{H^{k+4+\sigma}(\mathbb{T})})\rVert h\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}+(1+\rVert R\rVert_{H^{4}(\mathbb{T})})\rVert h^{(k+\sigma)}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}.

Similarly, we can obtain

L_{2}\lesssim(1+\rVert R\rVert_{H^{k+4+\sigma}(\mathbb{T})})\rVert h\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}+(1+\rVert R\rVert_{H^{4}(\mathbb{T})})\rVert h^{(k+\sigma)}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}.

Recalling (
4.4
), (
4.143
) and (
4.144
), we obtain (
4.140
). This proves (
4.137
) and finishes the proof.
∎

Proof of Proposition
4.13
:

If
i=j
, then the results follow immediately from Lemma
4.15
and
4.16
. For
i\neq j
, the same results follow straightforwardly since there is not singularity in the integrands.

\Box

In the next proposition, we estimate the derivative of the velocity with respect to the parameter
b
in view of (
4.5
).

Proposition 4.17

Let
R
,
z_{i}(R)
,
u_{i,j}
, be as in (
4.107
), (
4.108
) and (
4.109
). For
k\geq 2
, there exists
\epsilon=\epsilon(k,b_{1},b_{2})>0
such that if
\rVert R\rVert_{(H^{3}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\leq\epsilon
, then

\displaystyle\rVert\partial_{b_{1}}u_{i,j}(R)\rVert_{(H^{k}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\lesssim 1+\rVert R\rVert_{(H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}.

(4.151)

Also, if
\rVert R\rVert_{(H^{3}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}},\rVert r\rVert_{(H^{3}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\leq\epsilon
and
\rVert R\rVert_{(H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}},\rVert r\rVert_{(H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\leq 1
, then,

\displaystyle\rVert\partial_{b_{1}}u_{i,j}(R)-\partial_{b_{1}}u_{i,j}(r)\rVert_{(H^{k}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\lesssim\rVert R-r\rVert_{(H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}.

(4.152)

If
i,j\neq 1
, then (
4.151
) and (
4.152
) follow trivially, since
u_{2,2}
is independent of
b_{1}
. As in Proposition
4.13
, we only deal with the case where
i=j=1
.

Lemma 4.18

Let
R\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})
and
u(R)
and
z(R)
be as in (
4.110
) and (
4.111
). For
k\geq 2
, there exists
\epsilon=\epsilon(k,b)>0
such that if
\rVert R\rVert_{(H^{3}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\leq\epsilon
, then

\displaystyle\rVert\partial_{b}u(R)\rVert_{(H^{k}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\lesssim 1+\rVert R\rVert_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})}.

(4.153)

Also, if
\rVert R\rVert_{H^{3}(\mathbb{T})},\rVert r\rVert_{H^{3}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\epsilon
and
\rVert R\rVert_{(H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}},\rVert r\rVert_{(H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\leq 1
, then,

\displaystyle\rVert\partial_{b_{1}}u(R)-\partial_{b_{1}}u(r)\rVert_{(H^{k}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\lesssim\rVert R-r\rVert_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})}.

(4.154)

Proof.

From (
4.4
), we have

\displaystyle\partial_{b}u(R)(x)

\displaystyle=\partial_{b}u_{L}(R)(x)+\partial_{b}u_{N}(R)(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}}\log(2-2\cos(x-y))(-\cos y,-\sin y)dy+\int_{\mathbb{T}}K_{4}(R)(x,y)z(R)^{\prime\perp}(y)dy

\displaystyle\ +\int_{\mathbb{T}}K_{5}(R)(x,y)(-\cos y,-\sin y)dy

\displaystyle=:B_{1}+B_{2}(R)+B_{3}(R)

where

\displaystyle K_{4}(R)(x,y):=G(R(x),R(y),J(R)(x,y)),

\displaystyle K_{5}(R)(x,y):=F(R(x),R(y),J(R)(x,y)),

\displaystyle G(u,v,w)=\partial_{b}F(u,v,w)=\frac{2b+u+v}{b^{2}+b(u+v)+uv+w^{2}},

\displaystyle F(u,v,w)=\log(b^{2}+b(u+v)+uv+w^{2}).

Again, using Lemma
A.8
, we have that for
\rVert R\rVert_{H^{3}(\mathbb{T})},\rVert r\rVert_{H^{3}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\epsilon
for sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\rVert K_{4}(R)\rVert_{H^{k}(\mathbb{T}^{2})},\rVert K_{5}(R)\rVert_{H^{k}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}\lesssim 1+\rVert R\rVert_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})},

\displaystyle\rVert K_{4}(R)-K_{4}(r)\rVert_{H^{k}(\mathbb{T}^{2})},\rVert K_{5}(R)-K_{5}(r)\rVert_{H^{k}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}\lesssim(1+\rVert R\rVert_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})}+\rVert r\rVert_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})})\rVert R-r\rVert_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})}.

Then (
4.153
) and (
4.154
) follow immediately from Lemma
A.11
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
4.17
:

The case
i=j=1
follows immediately from Lemma
4.18
. If
i\neq j
then the result follows straightforwardly in a similar manner.

\Box

Regarding (
4.10
), (
4.11
) and (
4.13
) (note that since
b_{3}
in (
4.1
) depends on
\Theta_{3}
we need to estimate the second derivative wit respect to
b_{3}
of
G
.), we need to estimate the higher derivatives of
u_{i,j}
.

Proposition 4.19

Let
R
,
z_{i}(R)
,
u_{i,j}
, be as in (
4.107
), (
4.108
) and (
4.109
). For
k\geq 2
, there exists
\epsilon=\epsilon(k,b_{1},b_{2})>0
such that if
\rVert R\rVert_{(H^{3}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\leq\epsilon
, then

\displaystyle\rVert D^{2}u_{i,j}(R)[h,h]\rVert_{(H^{k}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\lesssim_{k}(1+\rVert R\rVert_{(H^{k+2}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}})\rVert h\rVert_{(H^{k}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}^{2},

(4.155)

\displaystyle\rVert\partial_{b_{3}}D^{2}u_{i,j}(R)[h,h]\rVert_{(H^{k}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\lesssim_{k}(1+\rVert R\rVert_{(H^{k+2}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}})\rVert h\rVert_{(H^{k}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}^{2},

\displaystyle\rVert\partial_{b_{1}}Du_{i,j}(R)[h]\rVert_{(H^{k}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\lesssim_{k}(1+\rVert R\rVert_{(H^{k+2}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}})\rVert h\rVert_{(H^{k}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}},

\displaystyle\rVert\partial_{b_{1}b_{1}}Du_{i,j}(R)[h]\rVert_{(H^{k}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\lesssim_{k}(1+\rVert R\rVert_{(H^{k+2}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}})\rVert h\rVert_{(H^{k}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}.

Proof.

We also consider the
i=j=1
case only and briefly sketch the idea for (
4.155
), since the proof is almost identical to Lemma
4.16
and Lemma
4.18
. Adapting the setting in (
4.110
) and (
4.111
), the Gateaux second derivative of
u
can be written as a linear combination of the integral operators of the form

\int_{\mathbb{T}}K(R)(x,y)L(x,y)dy,

where
K(R)(x,y)=\tilde{K}(R(x),R(y),J(R)(x,y))
for some smooth function
\tilde{K}:\mathbb{R}^{3}\mapsto\mathbb{R}^{2}
, and
L(x,y)
is a product of two of
h(x)
,
h(y)
,
h^{\prime}(y)
and
J(h)(x,y)
. As before,
K
satisfies the estimates in (
4.127
), (
4.128
) and (
4.129
). Let us consider
L(x,y)=J(h)(x,y)^{2}
only, that is,

T(R)[h,h]:=\int_{\mathbb{T}}K(R)(x,y)J(h)(x,y)J(h)(x,y)dy.

Therefore, it follows from Lemma
A.13
that

\displaystyle\rVert T(R)[h,h]\rVert_{H^{k}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim\rVert K\rVert_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})}\rVert h\rVert_{H^{k}(\mathbb{T})}^{2}\lesssim(1+\rVert R\rVert_{H^{k+2}(\mathbb{T})})\rVert h\rVert_{H^{k}(\mathbb{T})}^{2},

where the last inequality follows from the fact that
K
satisfies (
4.127
). As mentioned, the other terms can be estimated in a similar manner and we omit the proofs.
∎

4.5
Checking the hypotheses in Theorem
4.7

In this subsection we will show the hypotheses in theorem
4.7
are satisfied. We will always assume that
2\leq k\in\mathbb{N}
is fixed. We denote
R:=(R_{1},R_{2},R_{3})\in\left(X^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})\cap C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})\right)^{3}
. Throughout the rest of the paper, we assume that

\displaystyle|\Theta_{3}^{*}-\Theta_{3}|+\|R\|_{(X^{3})^{3}}\leq\epsilon,

(4.156)

for some
\epsilon
, sufficiently small. Note that from (
4.1
), (
4.1
) and Lemma
4.24
, it follows that

\displaystyle 0<b_{3}(\Theta_{3},R)<b_{2},

(4.157)

for sufficiently small
\epsilon
in (
4.156
).

We recall our functional from (
2.3
) and (
4.6
) can be written as (since
b_{1},b_{2},\Theta_{1},\Theta_{2}
are fixed, we omit their dependence but mark the dependence of
\Theta_{3}
in the notations)

\displaystyle G(\Theta_{3},R)=\begin{pmatrix}G_{1}(\Theta_{3},R)\\
G_{2}(\Theta_{3},R)\\
G_{3}(\Theta_{3},R)\end{pmatrix}=\frac{1}{4\pi}\begin{pmatrix}\sum_{i=1}^{3}\Theta_{i}\left(R_{1}^{\prime}u^{\theta}_{i,1}-(b_{1}+R_{1})u^{r}_{i,1}\right)\\
\sum_{i=1}^{3}\Theta_{i}\left(R_{2}^{\prime}u^{\theta}_{i,2}-(b_{2}+R_{2})u^{r}_{i,2}\right)\\
\sum_{i=1}^{3}\Theta_{i}\left(R_{3}^{\prime}u^{\theta}_{i,3}-(b_{3}+R_{3})u^{r}_{i,3}\right)\end{pmatrix}=:\begin{pmatrix}R_{1}^{\prime}u^{\theta}_{1}-(b_{1}+R_{1})u^{r}_{1}\\
R_{2}^{\prime}u^{\theta}_{2}-(b_{2}+R_{2})u^{r}_{2}\\
R_{3}^{\prime}u^{\theta}_{3}-(b_{3}+R_{3})u^{r}_{3}\end{pmatrix},

(4.158)

where for
i,j,k\in\left\{1,2,3\right\}
,

\displaystyle u^{\theta}_{k}:=u^{\theta}_{k}(\Theta_{3},R)=\frac{1}{4\pi}\sum_{i=1}^{3}\Theta_{i}u^{\theta}_{i,k},\quad u^{r}_{k}:=u^{r}_{k}(\Theta_{3},R)=\frac{1}{4\pi}\sum_{i=1}^{3}\Theta_{i}u^{r}_{i,k},

\displaystyle u^{\theta}_{i,j}=u^{\theta}_{i,j}(b_{3}(\Theta_{3}),\Theta_{3},R)=u_{i,j}(b_{3}(\Theta_{3}),\Theta_{3},R)\cdot\begin{pmatrix}-\sin x\\
\cos x\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle u^{r}_{i,j}=u^{r}_{i,j}(b_{3}(\Theta_{3}),\Theta_{3},R)=u_{i,j}(b_{3}(\Theta_{3}),\Theta_{3},R)\cdot\begin{pmatrix}\cos x\\
\sin x\end{pmatrix},

and
u_{i,j}(b_{3}(\Theta_{3}),\Theta_{3},R)
is given by (
4.108
) and (
4.109
) and
b_{3}(\Theta_{3},R)
is given by (
4.1
). We also denote by
\omega(\Theta_{3},R),\Psi(\Theta_{3},R):\mathbb{R}^{2}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
the corresponding vorticity and its stream function, more precisely,

\displaystyle\omega(\Theta_{3},R)(p):=\sum_{i=1}^{3}\Theta_{i}1_{D_{i}}(p),\quad\Psi(\Theta_{3},R)(p):=\frac{1}{2\pi}\left(\omega(\Theta_{3},R)*\log|\cdot|\right)(p),\quad p\in\mathbb{R}^{2},

(4.159)

\displaystyle D_{i}:=\left\{r(\cos x,\sin x)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:r\leq b_{i}+R_{i}(x),\ x\in\mathbb{T}\right\}.

(4.160)

With the stream function, we can write
G
as

\displaystyle{G(\Theta_{3},R)(x)}=-\begin{pmatrix}\partial_{x}\left(\Psi(\Theta_{3},R)(z_{1}(R)(x))\right)\\
\partial_{x}\left(\Psi(\Theta_{3},R)(z_{2}(R)(x))\right)\\
\partial_{x}\left(\Psi(\Theta_{3},R)(z_{3}(R)(x))\right)\end{pmatrix}.

(4.161)

The derivative of the functional will be given as

\displaystyle DG(\Theta_{3},R)[h]

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}u_{1}^{\theta}&0&0\\
0&u_{2}^{\theta}&0\\
0&0&u_{3}^{\theta}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}h_{1}^{\prime}\\
h_{2}^{\prime}\\
h_{3}^{\prime}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\ +\begin{pmatrix}-h_{1}u_{1}^{r}(\Theta_{3},R)+R_{1}^{\prime}Du_{1}^{\theta}(\Theta_{3},R)[h]-(b_{1}+R_{1})Du_{1}^{r}(\Theta_{3},R)[h]\\
-h_{2}u_{2}^{r}(\Theta_{3},R)+R_{2}^{\prime}Du_{2}^{\theta}(\Theta_{3},R)[h]-(b_{2}+R_{2})Du_{2}^{r}(\Theta_{3},R)[h]\\
-h_{3}u_{3}^{r}(\Theta_{3},R)+R_{3}^{\prime}Du_{3}^{\theta}(\Theta_{3},R)[h]-(b_{3}+R_{3})Du_{3}^{r}(\Theta_{3},R)[h]\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\ +Db_{3}(\Theta_{3},R)[h]\begin{pmatrix}R_{1}^{\prime}\partial_{b_{3}}u^{\theta}_{1}-(b_{1}+R_{1})\partial_{b_{3}}u^{r}_{1}\\
R_{2}^{\prime}\partial_{b_{3}}u^{\theta}_{2}-(b_{2}+R_{2})\partial_{b_{3}}u^{r}_{2}\\
R_{3}^{\prime}\partial_{b_{3}}u^{\theta}_{3}-(b_{3}+R_{3})\partial_{b_{3}}u^{r}_{3}-u^{r}_{3}\end{pmatrix},

(4.162)

where
Db_{3}(\Theta_{3},R)[h]
is given in (
4.1
). We define the projection
P_{0}:C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})\mapsto C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})
as

P_{0}f=f-\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{T}}f(x)dx,

and the linear maps,

\displaystyle a(\Theta_{3},R)[h]:=\begin{pmatrix}P_{0}\left(h_{1}^{\prime}u^{\theta}_{1}(\Theta_{3},R)\right)\\
0\\
0\end{pmatrix},\quad A(\Theta_{3},R)[h]:=DG(\Theta_{3},R)[h]-a(\Theta_{3},R)[h].

(4.163)

Now, we start checking the hypotheses. The hypothesis (a) immediately follows from (
2.5
), (
4.5
) and (
4.6
).

4.5.1
Regularity of the functional
G

In this subsection, we use the estimates obtained in Subsection
4.4
to prove that the functional
G
defined in (
4.158
) satisfies the following proposition:

Proposition 4.20

Let
2\leq k\in\mathbb{N}
and
\Theta_{3}
and
R
satisfy (
4.156
). Then,
G:\mathbb{R}\times(X^{k+1})^{3}\mapsto(Y^{k})^{3}
is well-defined and (
4.8
)-(
4.13
) hold.

Proof.

Note that
G_{j}
is the tangential derivative of the stream function on the corresponding boundary
D_{j}
(see (
4.161
)). Thus,
P_{0}G_{j}(\Theta,R)=0
, that is, each
G_{j}
has zero mean. If the boundary of the patch
D_{i}
is given by by
R_{i}\in X^{k+1}
, then
\omega(\Theta_{3},R)
is invariant under
\frac{2\pi}{m}
rotation about the origin and reflection, therefore,
G_{j}
is also
\frac{2\pi}{m}
periodic and odd. Furthermore, it follows from (A) in Proposition
4.13
and Lemma
A.5
that

\displaystyle\rVert G(\Theta_{3},R)\rVert_{(H^{k}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{i,j=1,2,3}\left(\bigg\rVert u_{i,j}(\Theta_{3},R)\cdot\begin{pmatrix}-\sin x\\
\cos x\end{pmatrix}R_{j}^{\prime}\bigg\rVert_{H^{k}(\mathbb{T})}+\bigg\rVert u_{i,j}(\Theta_{3},R)\cdot\begin{pmatrix}\cos x\\
\sin x\end{pmatrix}(b_{j}+R_{j})\bigg\rVert_{H^{k}(\mathbb{T})}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+\rVert R\rVert_{(H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}},

which proves (
4.8
) and that
G:\mathbb{R}\times(X^{k+1})^{3}\mapsto(Y^{k})^{3}
is well-defined. For (
4.9
) - (
4.13
), the results follow from (A) and (B) in Proposition
4.13
and Proposition
4.17
and
4.19
straightforwardly.
∎

4.5.2
The Dirichlet-Neumann operator

Here, we aim to prove that the linear operator
a(\Theta_{3},R)
in (
4.163
) satisfies (
4.14
). Thanks to Lemma
4.3
, we know that at a stationary solution, the corresponding velocity outside the support of the vorticity must vanish. In the following proposition, we will prove this quantitatively by using the main idea of
[
33
,
15
]
.

Proposition 4.21

There exists
\eta>0
such that if
\rVert R_{1}\rVert_{H^{k+2}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\eta
, then

\|u^{\theta}_{1}(\Theta_{3},R)\|_{H^{k}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim_{k}\|G_{1}(\Theta_{3},R)\|_{H^{k}(\mathbb{T})}.

Proof.

We adapt the notations in (
4.159
), (
4.160
) and (
4.161
) for
\omega
,
\Psi
and
D_{i}
. Clearly we have (we omit the dependence of
\Theta_{3}
and
R
for simplicity)

\displaystyle u^{\theta}_{1}(\alpha)=\nabla^{\perp}\Psi(z_{1}(\alpha))\cdot\begin{pmatrix}-\sin\alpha\\
\cos\alpha\end{pmatrix},\quad G_{1}(\alpha)=\nabla\Psi(z_{1}(\alpha))\cdot z_{1}^{\prime}(\alpha),\quad\alpha\in\mathbb{T},

(4.164)

where
z_{1}
is as given in (
4.108
). Note that
\Psi
is harmonic in
D_{1}^{c}
. In addition, it follows from (
4.2
) that
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\omega(y)dy=0
, hence, for
x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
,

\displaystyle|\Psi(x)|\lesssim\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\omega(y)\log\frac{|x-y|}{|x|}dy\bigg|=O\left(\frac{1}{|x|}\right),

\displaystyle|\nabla\Psi(x)|\lesssim\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\omega(y)\left(\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|^{2}}-\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\right)dy\bigg|=O\left(\frac{1}{|x|^{2}}\right).

With the above decay rates, we can use integration by parts to obtain that for
x\in\partial D_{1}
,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\int_{D_{1}^{c}}\log|x-y|\Delta\Psi(y)dy

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\partial D_{1}}\log|x-y|^{2}\nabla\Psi(y)\cdot\vec{n}(y)d\sigma(y)+\int_{\partial D_{1}}\nabla_{y}(\log|x-y|)\cdot\vec{n}(y)(\Psi(y)-\Psi(x))d\sigma(y)

\displaystyle=:-\frac{1}{2}L_{1}+L_{2},

(4.165)

where
\vec{n}
denotes the outer normal vector on
\partial D_{1}
. By the change of variables,
x\mapsto z_{1}(\alpha)
, and
y\mapsto z_{1}(\beta)
, we obtain

\displaystyle L_{1}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}}\log|z_{1}(\alpha)-z_{1}(\beta)|^{2}\nabla\Psi(z_{1}(\beta))\cdot(-z^{\prime}_{1}(\beta)^{\perp})d\beta

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}}\log(2-2\cos(\alpha-\beta))\nabla\Psi(z_{1}(\beta))\cdot(-z^{\prime}_{1}(\beta)^{\perp})d\beta

\displaystyle\ +\int_{\mathbb{T}}\log\left(\frac{|z_{1}(\alpha)-z_{1}(\beta)|^{2}}{2-2\cos(\alpha-\beta)}\right)\nabla\Psi(z_{1}(\beta))\cdot(-z^{\prime}_{1}(\beta)^{\perp})d\beta

\displaystyle=:L_{11}+L_{12}.

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle L_{2}=\int_{\mathbb{T}}\frac{(z_{1}(\alpha)-z_{1}(\beta))\cdot z_{1}^{\prime}(\beta)^{\perp}}{|z_{1}(\alpha)-z_{1}(\beta)|^{2}}(\Psi(z_{1}(\beta))-\Psi(z_{1}(\alpha))d\beta.

Hence, we obtain

\displaystyle L_{11}=-L_{12}+2L_{2}.

(4.166)

We claim that

\displaystyle\|L_{11}\|_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})}\gtrsim\|\nabla\Psi(z_{1}(\cdot))\cdot(-z^{\prime}_{1}(\cdot)^{\perp})\|_{H^{k}(\mathbb{T})}

(4.167)

\displaystyle\|L_{12}\|_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim\|R_{1}\|_{H^{k+2}(\mathbb{T})}\|\nabla\Psi(z_{1}(\cdot))\cdot(-z^{\prime}_{1}(\cdot)^{\perp})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}

(4.168)

\displaystyle\|L_{2}\|_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim\|\nabla\Psi(z_{1}(\cdot))\cdot(z^{\prime}_{1}(\cdot))\|_{H^{k}(\mathbb{T})}.

(4.169)

Let us assume the above claims for a moment. Then it follows from the claims, (
4.156
) and (
4.166
) that for sufficiently small
\eta>0
in the hypothesis of the proposition, we have

\|\nabla\Psi(z_{1}(\cdot))\cdot(-z^{\prime}_{1}(\cdot)^{\perp})\|_{H^{k}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim\|\nabla\Psi(z_{1}(\cdot))\cdot(z^{\prime}_{1}(\cdot))\|_{H^{k}(\mathbb{T})}.

With this inequality, we can obtain

\displaystyle\|\nabla\Psi(z_{1}(\cdot))\|_{H^{k}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle=\bigg\rVert\nabla\Psi(z_{1}(\cdot))\cdot\frac{z^{\prime}_{1}(\cdot)^{\perp}}{|z_{1}^{\prime}(\cdot)|}\bigg\lVert_{H^{k}(\mathbb{T})}+\bigg\rVert\nabla\Psi(z_{1}(\cdot))\cdot\frac{z^{\prime}_{1}(\cdot)}{|z_{1}^{\prime}(\cdot)|}\bigg\rVert_{H^{k}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\rVert z_{1}^{\prime}\rVert_{H^{k}(\mathbb{T})}\left(\|\nabla\Psi(z_{1}(\cdot))\cdot{z^{\prime}_{1}(\cdot)^{\perp}}\|_{H^{k}(\mathbb{T})}+\|\nabla\Psi(z_{1}(\cdot))\cdot{z^{\prime}_{1}(\cdot)}\|_{H^{k}(\mathbb{T})}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla\Psi(z_{1}(\cdot))\cdot{z^{\prime}_{1}(\cdot)}\|_{H^{k}(\mathbb{T})},

where the first inequalities follows from
\frac{1}{c}<|z_{1}^{\prime}|<c
for some
c>0
and the second inequality follows from
\rVert z_{1}^{\prime}\rVert_{H^{k}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim\rVert R_{1}\rVert_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\eta
for some small
\eta
. under the assumption (
4.156
). Finally, recalling (
4.164
), we obtain the desired result.

To finish the proof, we need to prove the claims (
4.167
)-(
4.169
). (
4.167
) follows from Lemma
A.14
. To see (
4.168
), we observe that

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{|z_{1}(\alpha)-z_{1}(\beta)|^{2}}{2-2\cos(\alpha-\beta)}\right)=\log(1+\mathcal{K}(\alpha,\beta)),

where
\mathcal{K}(\alpha,\beta):={2(R_{1}(\alpha)+R_{1}(\beta))}+R_{1}(\alpha)R_{1}(\beta)+\left(\frac{R_{1}(\alpha)-R_{1}(\beta)}{2\sin(\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2})}\right)^{2}
. Thus, it is straightforward that (thanks to (
4.156
),
1+\mathcal{K}\geq c>0
for some
c>0
)

\|\log(1+\mathcal{K})\|_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}\lesssim\|\mathcal{K}\|_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}\lesssim\|R_{1}\|_{H^{k+2}(\mathbb{T})},

where the last inequality follows from Lemma
A.5
and Lemma
A.10
. Thus,

\displaystyle\|L_{12}\|_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg\rVert\log\left(\frac{|z_{1}(\alpha)-z_{1}(\beta)|^{2}}{2-2\cos(\alpha-\beta)}\right)\bigg\rVert_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}\|\nabla\Psi(z_{1}(\cdot))\cdot(-z^{\prime}_{1}(\cdot)^{\perp})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|R_{1}\|_{H^{k+2}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}\|\nabla\Psi(z_{1}(\cdot))\cdot(-z^{\prime}_{1}(\cdot)^{\perp})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})},

which yields (
4.168
). Lastly, in order to show (
4.169
), we rewrite
L_{2}
as

L_{2}=\int_{\mathbb{T}}g(\alpha,\beta)\frac{((\Psi(z_{1}(\beta))-M_{\Psi})-(\Psi(z_{1}(\alpha))-M_{\Psi})}{2\sin(\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2})}d\beta,

where

g(\alpha,\beta)=\frac{(z_{1}(\alpha)-z_{1}(\beta))\cdot z_{1}^{\prime}(\beta)^{\perp}}{2\sin(\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2})}\cdot\frac{2-2\cos(\alpha-\beta)}{|z_{1}(\alpha)-z_{1}(\beta)|^{2}},\quad M_{\Psi}:=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\Psi(z_{1}(\alpha))d\alpha.

From Lemma
A.10
, and
A.5
, we have

\|g\|_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}\lesssim\|R_{1}\|_{H^{k+2}(\mathbb{T})}.

Therefore, it follows from Lemma
A.12
that

\|L_{2}\|_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim\|(\Psi(z_{1}(R,\cdot))-M_{\Psi})\|_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim\|\nabla\Psi(z_{1}(R,\cdot))\cdot(z^{\prime}_{1}(R,\cdot))\|_{H^{k}(\mathbb{T})},

where the last inequality follows from Poincaré inequality. Hence (
4.169
) follows.
∎

4.5.3
Estimates on the linearized operator

Our goal here is to prove that
G
satisfies the hypotheses
(c)
,
(\tilde{c}-1)
and
(\tilde{c}-2)
in Theorem
4.7
. More precisely, we will prove the following proposition:

Proposition 4.22

Let
a(\Theta_{3},R)
and
A(\Theta_{3},R)
be as in (
4.163
). Then

I)

a(\Theta_{3},R)\in\mathcal{L}((X^{k+1})^{3},(Y^{k})^{3})
,
A(\Theta_{3},R)\in\mathcal{L}((X^{k+1})^{3},Y^{k+1}\times(Y^{k})^{2})
with the estimates (
4.14
) and (
4.15
).

II)

For
(\Theta_{3}^{1},R^{1}),\ (\Theta_{3}^{2},R^{2})\in I\times(V^{3}\cap(C^{\infty})^{3}),
(
4.16
) holds.

III)

For any even
\sigma\in\mathbb{N}\cup\left\{0\right\}
, there exists
0<\eta<1
such that if
\rVert R\rVert_{(H^{k+3}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}}\leq\eta
, and
A(\Theta_{3},R)[h]=z
for some
z\in(C^{\infty})^{3}
and
h\in\text{Ker}(A(\Theta^{*}_{3},0))^{\perp}
, then (
4.17
) holds.

Proof.

Proof of I).
By definition of
a(\Theta_{3},R)
in (
4.163
), we have
\int_{\mathbb{T}}a(\Theta_{3},R)[h](x)dx=0
. Furthermore, clearly,
h_{1}^{\prime}
is a
\frac{2\pi}{m}
-periodic function and odd. Using the invariance under rotation/reflection of the stream function, it follows straightforwardly that
u^{\theta}_{1}
, which is the radial derivative of the stream function on the outmost boundary, is also
\frac{2\pi}{m}
-periodic and even. Therefore,
a(\Theta_{3},R)[h]
is
\frac{2\pi}{m}
periodic and odd. (
4.14
) follows immediately from (
4.21
), and
a(\Theta_{3},R)\in\mathcal{L}((X^{k+1})^{3},(Y^{k})^{3})
. Similarly,
A(\Theta_{3},R)\in\mathcal{L}((X^{k+1})^{3},Y^{k+1}\times(Y^{k})^{2})
and (
4.15
) follows from (B) in Proposition
4.13
.

Proof of II).
Thanks to (C) in Proposition
4.13
and Proposition
4.17
, each term in
A(\Theta_{3},R)
is Lipschitz continuous with respect to
R\in(H^{k+3}(\mathbb{T})^{3})
. Furthermore,
A(\theta_{3},R)
and
b_{3}
depend on
\Theta_{3}
smoothly, therefore the result follows immediately.

Proof of III).
In order to prove
III
, we first claim that for each even
\sigma\in\mathbb{N}\cup\left\{0\right\}
, there exist
\eta
and a linear map
T(R):(C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}\mapsto(C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}
such that if
\rVert R\rVert_{(H^{k+3}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}}\leq\eta
, then

\displaystyle\left(\frac{d}{dx}\right)^{\sigma}A(\Theta_{3},R)[h]=A(\Theta_{3},R)[h^{(\sigma)}]+L^{\sigma}(R)[h],

(4.170)

\displaystyle\rVert L^{\sigma}(R)[h]\rVert_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})\times(H^{k}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\lesssim_{k,\sigma}\rVert h\rVert_{(H^{k+\sigma}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}}+(1+\rVert R\rVert_{(H^{k+4+\sigma}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}})\rVert h\rVert_{(H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}}.

(4.171)

Let us assume that the above claims are true for a moment and let us suppose

A(\Theta_{3},R)[h]=z,\quad\text{ for some }z\in(C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}))^{3},\ h\in\text{Ker}(A(\Theta_{3}^{*},0))^{\perp}\subset(H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}.

From (
4.156
),
II)
, Lemma
A.15
and the assumption that
\rVert R\rVert_{(H^{k+3}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}}\leq\eta
for some small enough
\eta
, we have that
A(\Theta_{3},R):\text{Ker}(A(\Theta_{3}^{*},0))^{\perp}\subset((H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}))^{3})\mapsto\text{Im}(A(\Theta,R))\subset H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})\times(H^{k}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}
is invertible and

\displaystyle\rVert A(\Theta_{3},R)^{-1}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}(\text{Im}(A(\Theta_{3},R)),\text{Ker}(A(\Theta_{3}^{*},0))^{\perp})}\lesssim 1.

(4.172)

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\rVert h\rVert_{(H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}}\lesssim\rVert z\rVert_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})\times(H^{k}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}.

(4.173)

Now for each even
\sigma\in\mathbb{N}\left\{0\right\}
, it follows from (
4.170
) that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{d}{dx}\right)^{\sigma}A(\Theta_{3},R)[h]=A(\Theta_{3},R)[h^{(\sigma)}]+L^{\sigma}(R)[h]=z^{(\sigma)}.

Thanks to Proposition
4.23
, we have
h^{(\sigma)}\in\text{Ker}(A(\Theta_{3}^{*},0))^{\perp}
, thus, it follows from (
4.172
) that

h^{(\sigma)}=A(\Theta_{3},R)^{-1}[z^{(\sigma)}-L^{\sigma}(R)[h]],

and

\displaystyle\rVert h^{(\sigma)}\rVert_{(H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\rVert z^{(\sigma)}-L^{\sigma}(R)[h]\rVert_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})\times(H^{k}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\rVert z\rVert_{H^{k+1+\sigma}(\mathbb{T})\times(H^{k+\sigma}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}+\rVert L^{\sigma}(R)[h]\rVert_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})\times(H^{k}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}.

(4.174)

From (
4.171
), we also have

\displaystyle\rVert L^{\sigma}(R)[h]\rVert_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})\times(H^{k}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\rVert h\rVert_{(H^{k+\sigma}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}}+(1+\rVert R\rVert_{(H^{k+4+\sigma}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}})\rVert h\rVert_{(H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}}.

(4.175)

Using Lemma
A.6
, we have

\displaystyle\rVert h\rVert_{(H^{k+\sigma}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\rVert h\rVert_{(H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}}+\bigg\rVert\left(h^{(k+1)}\right)^{(\sigma-1)}\bigg\rVert_{(L^{2}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\rVert h\rVert_{(H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}}+\left(\rVert h^{(k+1)}\rVert_{(L^{2}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}}\right)^{\frac{1}{\sigma}}\bigg\rVert\left(h^{(k+1)}\right)^{(\sigma)}\bigg\rVert_{(L^{2}{(\mathbb{T}))^{3}}}^{\frac{\sigma-1}{\sigma}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+C(\epsilon))\rVert\rVert h\rVert_{(H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}}+\epsilon\rVert h^{(\sigma)}\rVert_{(H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}},

for any
\epsilon>0
, which follows from Young’s inequality. Plugging this into (
4.175
) and using (
4.173
), we obtain

\rVert L^{\sigma}(R)[h]\rVert_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})\times(H^{k}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}\lesssim(C(\epsilon)+\rVert R\rVert_{(H^{k+4+\sigma}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}})\rVert z\rVert_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})\times(H^{k}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}+\epsilon\rVert h^{(\sigma)}\rVert_{(H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}}

Hence we choose sufficiently small
\epsilon
depending on
k
and
\sigma
so that (
4.5.3
) yields that

\rVert h^{(\sigma)}\rVert_{(H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}}\lesssim\rVert z\rVert_{H^{k+1+\sigma}(\mathbb{T})\times(H^{k+\sigma}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}+(1+\rVert R\rVert_{(H^{k+4+\sigma}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}})\rVert z\rVert_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})\times(H^{k}(\mathbb{T}))^{2}}.

With this estimate and (
4.173
), (
4.17
) follows.

In order to finish the proof, we need to prove the claim (
4.170
) and (
4.171
), we note that each component of
A(\Theta_{3},R)[h]
consists of linear combination of the following forms (see (
4.1
), (
4.5
) and (
4.163
)):

\displaystyle u^{\theta}_{i}(R)h_{i}^{\prime},\text{ for }i=2,3,

\displaystyle h_{i}u_{i}^{r}(\Theta_{3},R),\ R_{i}^{\prime}Du^{\theta}_{i}(\Theta_{3},R)[h],\ (b_{i}+R_{i})Du_{1}^{r}(\Theta_{3},R)[h],\ \text{ for }i=1,2,3.

(4.176)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}}R_{i}(x)h_{i}(x)dx\left(R_{i}^{\prime}\partial_{b_{3}}u^{\theta}_{i}-(b_{i}+R_{i})\partial_{b_{3}}u_{i}^{r}\right),\ \text{ for i=1,2}

(4.177)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}}R_{3}(x)h_{3}(x)dx\left(R_{3}^{\prime}\partial_{b_{3}}u^{\theta}_{3}-(b_{3}+R_{3})\partial_{b_{3}}u_{3}^{r}-u_{3}^{r}\right).

(4.178)

For
i=2,3
, it is clear that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{d}{dx}\right)^{\sigma}\left(u^{\theta}_{i}(R)h^{\prime}_{i}\right)=u^{\theta}_{i}h^{(\sigma+1)}_{i}+\sum_{p+q=\sigma,\ q\leq\sigma-1}C_{p,q,\sigma}\underbrace{\left(u^{\theta}_{i}(R)\right)^{(p)}h_{i}^{(q+1)}}_{L^{\sigma}_{1}(R)[h_{i}]},

(4.179)

for some
C_{p,q,\sigma}
. From Lemma
A.7
, we have

\displaystyle\rVert L^{\sigma}_{1}(R)[h_{i}]\rVert_{H^{k}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\rVert u_{i}^{\theta}(R)\rVert_{H^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\rVert h_{i}\rVert_{(H^{k+\sigma}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}}+\rVert u_{i}^{\theta}(R)\rVert_{H^{k+\sigma}(\mathbb{T})}\rVert h_{i}\rVert_{(H^{1}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\rVert h_{i}\rVert_{H^{k+\sigma}(\mathbb{T})}+(1+\rVert R\rVert_{(H^{k+1+\sigma}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}})\rVert h_{i}\rVert_{H^{1}(\mathbb{T})},

(4.180)

where the second inequality follows from Proposition
4.13
.
For the other terms, we only deal with
R_{1}^{\prime}Du_{1}^{\theta}(\Theta_{3},R)[h]
, since the other terms can be dealt with in the same way. Thus, we will show that there exists a linear operator
L_{2}^{\sigma}(R)[h]
such that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{d}{dx}\right)^{\sigma}\left(R_{1}^{\prime}Du_{1}^{\theta}(\Theta_{3},R)[h]\right)=R_{1}^{\prime}Du_{1}^{\theta}(\Theta_{3},R)[h^{(\sigma)}]+L_{2}^{\sigma}(R)[h],

(4.181)

\displaystyle\rVert L_{2}^{\sigma}(R)[h]\rVert_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim_{k,\sigma}\rVert h\rVert_{(H^{k+\sigma}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}}+\rVert R\rVert_{(H^{k+4+\sigma}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}}\rVert h\rVert_{(H^{3}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}}.

(4.182)

It follows from (D) in Proposition
4.13
that there exists
T^{\sigma}(R)[h]
such that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{d}{dx}\right)^{\sigma}\left(R_{1}^{\prime}Du_{1}^{\theta}(\Theta_{3},R)[h]\right)

\displaystyle=R_{1}^{\prime}\left(Du_{1}^{\theta}(\Theta_{3},R)[h]\right)^{(\sigma)}+\sum_{p+q=\sigma,\ q\leq\sigma-1}(R_{1}^{\prime})^{(p)}\left(Du_{1}^{\theta}(\Theta_{3},R)[h]\right)^{(q)}

\displaystyle=R_{1}^{\prime}Du_{1}^{\theta}(\Theta_{3},R)[h^{(\sigma)}]+\underbrace{R_{1}^{\prime}T^{\sigma}(R)[h]+\sum_{p+q=\sigma,\ q\leq\sigma-1}C_{p,q,\sigma}(R_{1}^{\prime})^{(p)}\left(Du_{1}^{\theta}(\Theta_{3},R)[h]\right)^{(q)}}_{=:L^{\sigma}_{2}(R)[h]},

and

\rVert T^{\sigma}(R)[h]\rVert_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim(1+\rVert R\rVert_{(H^{4}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}})\rVert h^{(k+\sigma)}\rVert_{(L^{2}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}}+(1+\rVert R\rVert_{(H^{k+4+\sigma}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}})\rVert h\rVert_{(L^{2}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}}.

Therefore, from (B) in Proposition
4.13
and Lemma
A.7
, it follows that

\displaystyle\rVert R_{1}^{\prime}T^{\sigma}(R)[h]\rVert_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\rVert R_{1}^{\prime}\rVert_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})}\rVert T^{\sigma}(R)[h]\rVert_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\rVert h^{(k+\sigma)}\rVert_{(L^{2}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}}+(1+\rVert R\rVert_{(H^{k+4+\sigma}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}})\rVert h\rVert_{(L^{2}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}},

(4.183)

where we used that
H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})
is a Banach algebra and that
\rVert R\rVert_{(H^{4}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}}\lesssim\rVert R\rVert_{(H^{k+3}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}}\leq\eta
for some
0<\eta<1
. From Lemma
A.7
, we also have

\displaystyle\rVert(R_{1}^{\prime})^{(p)}\left(Du_{1}^{\theta}(\Theta_{3},R)[h]\right)^{(q)}\rVert_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\rVert R_{1}\rVert_{H^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\rVert Du_{1}^{\theta}(\Theta_{3},R)[h]\rVert_{H^{k+\sigma}(\mathbb{T})}+\rVert R_{1}\rVert_{H^{k+1+\sigma}(\mathbb{T})}\rVert Du_{1}^{\theta}(\Theta_{3},R)[h]\rVert_{H^{1}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\rVert Du_{1}^{\theta}(\Theta_{3},R)[h]\rVert_{H^{k+\sigma}(\mathbb{T})}+\rVert R_{1}\rVert_{H^{k+1+\sigma}(\mathbb{T})}\rVert Du_{1}^{\theta}(\Theta_{3},R)[h]\rVert_{H^{3}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\rVert h\rVert_{(H^{k+\sigma}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}}+\rVert R\rVert_{(H^{k+3+\sigma}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}}\rVert h\rVert_{(H^{1}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}}

\displaystyle\ +\rVert R_{1}\rVert_{H^{k+1+\sigma}(\mathbb{T})}\rVert Du_{1}^{\theta}(\Theta_{3},R)[h]\rVert_{H^{3}(\mathbb{T})}

where the last inequality follows from (B) in Proposition
4.13
,which also tells us that

\displaystyle\rVert Du_{1}^{\theta}(\Theta_{3},R)[h]\rVert_{H^{3}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+\rVert R\rVert_{(H^{5}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}})\rVert h\rVert_{(H^{3}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+\rVert R\rVert_{(H^{k+3+\sigma}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}})\rVert h\rVert_{(H^{3}(\mathbb{T})^{3})},

where we used
k\geq 2
.
Therefore, we have

\rVert(R_{1}^{\prime})^{(p)}\left(Du_{1}^{\theta}(\Theta_{3},R)[h]\right)^{(q)}\rVert_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim\rVert h\rVert_{(H^{k+\sigma}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}}+(1+\rVert R\rVert_{(H^{k+3+\sigma}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}})\rVert h\rVert_{(H^{3}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}}.

Combining with (
4.5.3
), we obtain (
4.182
). Since every term in (
4.176
), (
4.177
) and (
4.178
) can be shown to satisfy the same property as in (
4.181
) and (
4.182
), combining with (
4.179
) and (
4.180
), we obtain (
4.170
) and (
4.171
).

∎

4.5.4
Spectral Study

In this subsection, we will verify the hypotheses
(d)
and
(e)
. They will be proved in Proposition
4.23
and Lemma
4.26
respectively.

Proposition 4.23

Let
2\leq m\in\mathbb{N}.
We can choose
b_{1}=\Theta_{1}=1
and
b_{2}
and
\Theta_{2}
be as in (
4.1
). Also we set
b_{3}
as in (
4.1
). Then, there exists
\Theta^{*}_{3}>0
such that
A(\Theta^{*}_{3},0)\in\mathcal{L}((H^{k+1})^{3},H^{k+1}\times(H^{k})^{2})
has one-dimensional kernel and codimension one. That is,

\displaystyle\text{Ker}(A(\Theta^{*}_{3},0))=\text{span}\left\{v\right\},\quad\text{Im}(A(\Theta^{*}_{3},0))^{\perp}=\text{span}\left\{w\right\}.

Furthermore,
0<b_{3}(\Theta^{*}_{3},0)<b_{2}<b_{1}=1
and
v
and
w
are supported on the
m
-th Fourier mode.

The proof of the above proposition will be achieved after proving several lemmas.

Let us first compute the derivative of
G
at
(\Theta_{3},0)
. Since we have
G(\Theta_{3},0)=0
for any
\Theta_{3}
,
I
in Proposition
4.22
yields that
a(\Theta_{3},0)=0
for any
\Theta_{3}
. Moreover, it follows from (
4.5
), (
4.1
) and Lemma
3.5
that letting
h_{1}(x)=\sum_{n\geq 1}h_{1,n}\cos(mnx),

h_{2}(x)=\sum_{n\geq 1}h_{2,n}\cos(mnx)
,
h_{3}(x)=\sum_{n\geq 1}h_{3,n}\cos(mnx)
,

\displaystyle A(\Theta_{3},0)[h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}]=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}Q_{1}(x)\\
Q_{2}(x)\\
Q_{3}(x)\end{array}\right),

where

\displaystyle Q_{1}(x)=\sum_{n\geq 1}q_{1,n}\sin(mnx),\quad Q_{2}(x)=\sum_{n\geq 1}q_{2,n}\sin(mnx),\quad Q_{3}(x)=\sum_{n\geq 1}q_{3,n}\sin(mnx)

where the coefficients satisfy, for any
n
:

\displaystyle(-mn)M_{n}(\Theta_{3},b_{3})\left(\begin{array}[]{c}h_{1,n}\\
h_{2,n}\\
h_{3,n}\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}q_{1,n}\\
q_{2,n}\\
q_{3,n}\end{array}\right),

with

\displaystyle M_{n}(\Theta_{3},b_{3})=

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\left(-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2mn}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\Theta_{2}b_{2}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\Theta_{3}b_{3}^{2}&\Theta_{2}b_{2}\frac{b_{2}^{mn}}{2mn}&\Theta_{3}b_{3}\frac{b_{3}^{mn}}{2mn}\\
\frac{b_{2}^{mn}}{2mn}&\Theta_{2}b_{2}\left(-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2mn}\right)+\left(-\frac{1}{2}b_{2}\right)+\left(-\frac{1}{2}\right)\Theta_{3}\frac{b_{3}^{2}}{b_{2}}&\Theta_{3}b_{3}\frac{1}{2mn}\left(\frac{b_{3}}{b_{2}}\right)^{mn}\\
\frac{b_{3}^{mn}}{2mn}&\Theta_{2}b_{2}\frac{1}{2mn}\left(\frac{b_{3}}{b_{2}}\right)^{mn}&\Theta_{3}b_{3}\left(-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2mn}\right)+\left(-\frac{1}{2}b_{3}\right)-\frac{\Theta_{2}b_{3}}{2}\end{array}\right).

Lemma 4.24

Let
2\leq m\in\mathbb{N}
, and
b_{2},

\Theta_{2}
satisfy (
4.1
). Then, there exists
\Theta^{*}_{3}:=\Theta^{*}_{3,m}>0
and
b_{3}
such that
0<b_{3}<b_{2},
and

\displaystyle\text{det}(M_{1})=0,\ \Theta_{1}b_{1}^{2}+\Theta_{2}b_{2}^{2}+\Theta_{3}^{*}b_{3}^{2}=1+\Theta_{2}b_{2}^{2}+\Theta_{3}^{*}b_{3}^{2}=0,

(4.193)

\displaystyle\text{det}(M_{n}(\Theta^{*}_{3},b_{3}))\neq 0,\quad\text{ if $n\geq 2$.}

(4.194)

Proof.

We first write
\text{det}(M_{1})
as a polynomial in
b_{3}
. Under the constraint
1+\Theta_{2}b_{2}^{2}+\Theta_{3}b_{3}^{2}=0
, we get

\displaystyle M_{1}(\Theta_{3},b_{3})

\displaystyle=\small\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\frac{1}{2m}&\Theta_{2}b_{2}\frac{b_{2}^{m}}{2m}&\Theta_{3}b_{3}\frac{b_{3}^{m}}{2m}\\
\frac{b_{2}^{m}}{2m}&\Theta_{2}b_{2}\left(-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2m}\right)+\left(-\frac{1}{2}b_{2}\right)+\left(-\frac{1}{2}\right)\Theta_{3}\frac{b_{3}^{2}}{b_{2}}&\Theta_{3}b_{3}\frac{1}{2m}\left(\frac{b_{3}}{b_{2}}\right)^{m}\\
\frac{b_{3}^{m}}{2m}&\Theta_{2}b_{2}\frac{1}{2m}\left(\frac{b_{3}}{b_{2}}\right)^{m}&\Theta_{3}b_{3}\left(-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2m}\right)+\left(-\frac{1}{2}b_{3}\right)+\left(-\frac{1}{2}\right)\Theta_{2}b_{3}\end{array}\right).

Therefore, multiplying the first row by
2m
, multiplying the third column by
b_{3}
and dividing the second column by
b_{2}
, we get

\displaystyle\frac{2mb_{3}}{b_{2}}\text{det}(M_{1}(\Theta_{3},b_{3}))=\text{det}\small\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}1&\Theta_{2}b_{2}^{m}&\Theta_{3}b_{3}^{2}b_{3}^{m}\\
\frac{b_{2}^{m}}{2m}&\Theta_{2}\left(-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2m}\right)+\left(-\frac{1}{2}\right)+\left(-\frac{1}{2}\right)\Theta_{3}\frac{b_{3}^{2}}{b_{2}^{2}}&\Theta_{3}\frac{1}{2m}b_{3}^{2}\left(\frac{b_{3}}{b_{2}}\right)^{m}\\
\frac{b_{3}^{m}}{2m}&\Theta_{2}\frac{1}{2m}\left(\frac{b_{3}}{b_{2}}\right)^{m}&\Theta_{3}b_{3}^{2}\left(-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2m}\right)+\left(-\frac{1}{2}b_{3}^{2}\right)+\left(-\frac{1}{2}\right)\Theta_{2}b_{3}^{2}\end{array}\right)
